# The Lean library `MassGap`

This is the map of the Lean library in `research/lean/` for readers of the Lean. The paper's section 11 (`research/paper/v3/11-formal.md`) states the trust base, the build and the table of its numbered theorems; the files `research/paper/v3/*.lean-map.md` give the declarations and open hypotheses of every numbered theorem of sections 1 to 9. This file covers the rest of the library: what each part is for, its capstones, its open hypotheses, its modules, and which declarations not to use.

## Conventions

- Every declaration is in the namespace `MassGap.<Module>`, with `<Module>` the file name, except where a file is named. The prefix `MassGap.` is omitted below.
- The Lean coupling is the coefficient of the ordered-plaquette action: `β = β_W/2`.
- Provenance of the one-line statements. A line marked (sig) is read against the Lean signature, as of the paper's lean-map files. Every other line is taken from the module docstring or from the module inventory; its declaration exists by name, and its signature is not read here. Treat unmarked lines as pointers, and read the signature before citing one.
- "Open" means no declaration in the library concludes the statement.
- The module lists are grouped by content. `research/lean/MassGap.lean` imports the modules in build order, not by topic, and its import comments are not a reliable description of the modules.

## Trust base and build

Lean `v4.31.0`, Mathlib tag `v4.31.0` (commit `fabf563a7c95a166b8d7b6efca11c8b4dc9d911f`), pinned in `research/lean/lake-manifest.json`. 720 modules, about 428,000 lines. No `sorry`, no `axiom` declaration, no `native_decide`. Every declaration with a recorded footprint depends on a subset of `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; the footprints are produced by `research/code/certify/lean_axiom_footprints.py` into `research/data/13_dat_axiom_footprints.csv` (13,752 rows, no other axiom). Build:

```
cd research/lean
lake exe cache get
lake build
```

## The three Clay propositions

Three propositions in the library carry the name "Clay". They are different statements and none implies another.

| Proposition | Couplings | What it states | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ClayStatement` (file `ClayStatementG.lean`), `= ∀ N ≥ 2, ClayStatementG.ClayStatementOf (SU N)` (`ClayStatementG.clayStatement_iff_SU`, `Iff.rfl`) | a measured tower `MeasuredTower.MeasTowerG` | Osterwalder–Schrader axioms OS0 to OS4 on admissible families of separated test functions, for the energy-momentum tensor and all gauge-invariant local composites; a reconstructed Hamiltonian with a gap on the tensor's space and on the composites; asymptotic freedom of the trace two-point kernel; non-Gaussianity; the translation Ward identity; limits along one fixed non-principal ultrafilter. 32 conjuncts in `ClayStatementG.ClayDataP`. | the target; Theorem 1.1 of the paper concludes it |
| `ClayFull` (file `ClayFull.lean`) | the two-loop tower `dyBeta` | for one scalar field (the energy density read through a flow), Schwinger functions on Schwartz test functions, `SO(4)` and translation invariance, OS2 and OS3, a self-adjoint Hamiltonian with a gap, non-Gaussianity, a linked local composite | not used by the paper; no tensor, no composites |
| `ClayRoutes.ClayContinuum`, `ClayRoutes.ClayContinuumPair` | the two-loop tower `dyBeta` | at every dyadic step a gap at physical rate `c/L` of the reconstructed continuum transfer data, and a non-trivial vacuum; no Osterwalder–Schrader axiom in the conclusion | conclusion of the box route (`clay15`) and of the existence-side routes `clay_canonical`, `clay_final`, `clay_two`; the paper's `𝒞(L)` |

The target is `ClayStatement`. Its own docstring lists what it does not say: other groups than `SU(N)` are in `ClayStatementG` (`ClayStatementSO` through `ClayStatementE8`); the Schwinger functions are on separated families, with no extension to coincident points; the limit is along an ultrafilter; the reconstruction to Wightman fields is not formalized; the Weyl channel and composites above dimension four are tied, not normalized. `ClayFull`'s docstring states that `ApertureVolume.clay_two`, which concludes `ClayContinuumPair`, supplies no clause of `ClayFull`.

## Cluster 1. The doubling defect, the bootstrap, the coupling tower and the root

Purpose. The doubling defect of the periodic Wilson torus, `windowCoupling N β w = 16 log Z(2^w) − log Z(2^{w+1})`, read, as an argued interpretation, as a Rényi-2 entropy of the transfer operator. A squaring recursion turns one window with defect at most `1/1000` into vanishing at every larger dyadic torus, and vanishing gives a size-uniform transfer gap. This is a form of the known purity bootstrap (thermal Rényi-2), from OpenAI's two-dimensional O(3) and O(4) work and periodic spin-one Haldane gap proof (github.com/openai/math, commit adc7f12) and Grover's extensions of that proof (arXiv:2611.07400), all cited in the paper; it is not claimed as new. The bridge statement `CouplingTower.StrongEnd N` asks for vanishing at arbitrarily large `β`; the root theorem derives the target from it and two further inputs. This cluster holds the gap half of the argument (paper sections 4 and 5).

Capstones.

- `DoublingSharp.rec_sharp`: `Δ ≤ 1/1000 ⇒ Δ(next) ≤ 250 Δ²` (sig). `DoublingSharp.iterate_sharp`: `250 Δ(0) ≤ 1/4 ⇒ 250 Δ(k) ≤ (1/4)^{2^k}` (sig).
- `DoublingPower.defect_squaring`: squaring from the four trace facts, without an eigenvalue list (sig). `RectTransfer.ZR_eq_traceSeq`: the trace identity (sig).
- `DoublingSharp.wilson_gap_sharp`: size-uniform gap of the kernel operator from a seed window (sig).
- `CouplingTower.tendsto_zero_of_seed`, `CouplingTower.strongEnd_iff_seed`: one seed window gives vanishing; `StrongEnd N` iff seed windows at arbitrarily large `β` (sig).
- `CouplingTower.windowGap`: gap of the gauge-invariant transfer of the periodic state at rate `log 2 / 2^w` (sig).
- `WeakEndProved.weakEnd`, `TwistedTraceBoundN.defect_gt` (file `TwistedTraceBoundFull.lean`): on a fixed torus the defect exceeds `1/1000` at every large coupling, `N ≥ 2` (sig).
- `StrongAnchor.strongAnchor`: vanishing at small coupling (sig). `WeakEndProved.strongEnd_iff_exists_couplingTower'`: for `0 < u₀ ≤ 1/1000` a coupling tower exists exactly when (S) holds (sig).
- `OneInput.clayStatement_of_strongEnd`: `ClayStatementOf (SU N)` from `StrongEnd N` and, frequently near `0⁺` along every coupling tower, `MeasTowerG` (band level existential per tower) and `ExistAlong` (sig). This is Theorem 1.1.
- `ExistClose.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_open'`: the existence input in the concrete form `OpenClose` (sig).
- `ReflFloor.headline_hypotheses_inconsistent`, `ReflFloor.not_floor_residual`, `ReflFloor.not_upper_residual`: under `StrongEnd N` the window reading of a tower grows like `log(1/(250 u₀))`, so every fixed-level form of the upper band is refuted (sig). `MassBand.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_deep`: the depth-graded form of Theorem 1.1.
- `ReadPos.gE_zero_pos`: the contact reflected read of the tensor is strictly positive at every coupling (sig). `FinalBridge.windowReading_floor`: the lower half of the measured-mass band (sig).
- `ClayGFinal.clayStatementOf_of_noNTAlong`: the Hamiltonian and spectral gaps, the non-triviality clauses, OS2, OS4, the link and observer generation, from a tower and the existence fields (sig).
- `StrongAnchor.strongEnd_of_noStall`; `DisclosureFloor.noStall_iff_noCriticalPoint`; `FloorTension.noStall_iff_flux_zeroFlux`: equivalent forms of the everywhere statement `NoStall`, which is stronger than (S).

Open hypotheses. `CouplingTower.StrongEnd N`; `MeasuredTower.MeasTowerG (SU N) βs` along every coupling tower; `ConstantPhysicsLine.ExistAlong N βs` along every coupling tower (its concrete witness form `ExistClose.OpenClose`; the class moments in it are proved only from the stronger `TowerOpen.SiteExpG`). No declaration concludes any of the three, and none shows `ExistAlong` satisfiable at any tower. `FinalBridge.strongEnd_of_massReadOpens` reaches `StrongEnd` only under `MassGivesVanishing`, a named template, not a standard theorem.

Modules.

- Bootstrap and decay: `DoublingRecursion`, `DoublingSharp`, `DoublingPower`, `KernelPower`, `KernelEigen`, `KernelGap`, `CyclicKernel`, `RectWilson`, `RectTransfer`, `DoublingWilson`, `PeriodicDyadic`, `TorusIdentify`, `HiddenChain`, `TorusChain`, `ChainClosing`, `ChainReduce`, `ChainDecay`, `TorusDecay`, `DoublingClay2`, `ZoomSelfScale`, `TorusDoubling`, `EffectiveCoupling`, `EnergySeed`.
- Sector seeds and the centre flux (`SU(2)` and `SU(N)`): `FluxSectorBound`, `TwistedTraceBound`, `SectorDefect`, `CentreSectorSeed`, `FluxShareSquaring`, `FluxSectorFourier`, `TwistedTraceBoundN`, `ZeroFluxSector`, `SliceCentreAction`, `CentreSectorSeedN`, `CentreSectorSeedTwo`, `TwistDeficitUnionN`, `TwistedTraceBoundFull`, `GapSideTwistFlat`, `GapSideTwistBridge`, `GapSideVortexAntiDiagonal`, `GapSideVortexStepSign`, `GapSideVortexRect`, `GapSideCrossover`, `GapSideSheetPolymer`, `GapSideSheetPolymerWrap`, `GapSideExactDecimation`, `ConvolutionTransfer`, `HolderBondMoving`, `ChainCoarseSeed`, `ChainFirstCrossing`, `WilsonChainExists`, `ElectricStep`, `ElectricStepChain`, `SU2DiskLaw`, `MovedTorusDecimation`, `ElectricExactProof`.
- Tower, bridge and root: `CouplingTower`, `Telescope`, `WeakEndProved`, `StrongEndVanishing`, `TowerParamFree`, `SelfSimilarStep`, `ToronDecrement`, `LineSeedsVanishing`, `LineSeedsBasin`, `CasimirStep`, `ReferenceUnit`, `FluxSplitCasimir`, `RunningCrossover`, `ConstantPhysicsLine`, `ConsolidatedRoot`, `LinkAlongG`, `TowerOpen`, `CouplingGen`, `FlowAlong`, `FieldSweep`, `SunFamFlux`, `ObserverTower`, `BlFamCore`, `ObserverTowerPM`, `ObserverTowerCL`, `GapReadsTensorProved`, `ClayMeasDirect`, `SchedAlong`, `StrongAnchor`, `DisclosureFloor`, `FloorSelfSim`, `FloorTension`, `FloorModes`, `FocusFloor`, `FinalBridge`, `OneInput`, `ReadSaturates`, `BandUpper`, `StepPeriodic`, `BandClose`, `ExistClose`, `ReadPos`, `PlaqIndep`, `RegNontrivial`, `ReflFloor`, `MassBand`, `TransferBand`, `NextWindow`, `BasinGrowth`, `ZoomContract`, `FlatCap`, `FluxFold`, `WindowRead`, `DeltaLengthTower`.
- Window and zoom readings: `ScaleLightCone`, `ZoomFree`, `ApertureModes`, `ApertureCount`, `ApertureThing`, `ExpandMatch`, `ProxyCov`, `RoutePin`, `FreeLevels`, `ModeHyper`, `FreeBridge`, `ZoomBase`, `CoefPin`, `ProxBound`.
- The count and the screen (paper section 3): `RenyiOrder`, `CountMoments`, `ApertureFinite`, `BareRatioProof`, `PlaqPositive`, `PlaqShortDistance`, `PlaqSummable`, `ModeAzuma`, `ApertureSpectral`, `FractalTail`, `ZoomDown`.
- Further modules of this cluster, one line each.
  - `PlaqSummable`: toward the last clause of `PlaqShortDistance.SplitRatio`; a bounded tilt moves a variance ratio by at most `(B/A)²` (`tvar_le`, `var_le_tvar`, `relVar_transfer`), and the short-distance part of the row sum costs the ball count `n` at every coupling and every state (`opNorm_of_shortTail`, `ratio_of_shortTail`) (sig). Open: the tail bound and the per-link floor (hypotheses `hτF`, `hdF`); not instantiated at `ClayTreeG.schedSU N`.
  - `ModeAzuma`: sub-Gaussian moments along the link martingale of the Wilson state, at every `N ≠ 0`, every real `β` and every observable; Azuma–Hoeffding for the filtration that releases one link at a time (`mgf_chain`, `moment_chain`), the increment bound on a sparse chain (`moment_sparse`), and the centred moment in the shape of `CountMoments.ModeHyperG` (`moment_centred`, `modeMoment_of_azuma`, under `32 V ≤ C λ`) (sig). Open: that comparison with one `C` for every unit eigenvector along the tower; the module text argues that the ratio grows along the tower and is bounded only at strong coupling.
  - `ApertureSpectral`: the long-lag half of `BareRatioProof.LowModeRatio` for a finite spectral sum in one direction; the Poisson kernel and its positive mixtures peak at zero momentum and keep half the peak on the gap window (`kerP_le`, `kerP_half`, `mix_le`, `mix_half`), and one fixed physical screen side puts the lowest Dirichlet momentum inside the gap window at every step of `MeasuredTower.schedAlongG` (`lowFreq_le`, `lowFreq_le_gap`) (sig). Open: `LowModeRatio` itself, the combination with the short lags.
  - `FrobeniusUnitary`: the Frobenius norm of `N × N` complex matrices is invariant under a unitary factor on either side (`frob_unitary_mul`, `frob_mul_unitary`) (sig); `frob_sq` gives `‖X‖² = Re tr(Xᴴ X)`. No open hypothesis.
  - `HolonomyLadder`: the lattice Stokes ladder; the ordered product of unitaries along one path differs from the rung-conjugated product along a parallel path by at most the sum of the plaquette distances from `1`, and the traces differ by at most `N` times that sum (`ladder`, `abs_trace_sub_le_of_ladder`); `norm_sub_one_sq` gives `‖g − 1‖² = 2 N · wilsonDensity g` on `SU(N)` (sig). No open hypothesis.
  - `ChargedWordTrace`: nearly commuting unitaries have a charged word of large trace; `exists_charged_of_commute` gives a charged index when `X Y = Y X`, and `commSize_le` bounds the commutator size by `N²` times the commutator norm (sig). Feeds the centre-sector seeds. No open hypothesis.
  - `SeedZeroCoupling`: the doubling seed holds at zero coupling; `ZR_at_zero` (`ZR = 1` at `β = 0`), `defect_at_zero` (the defect is `0`), `seed_at_zero` (sig). No open hypothesis.
  - `WDeltaRun`: the seed `DoublingClay2.WDelta2` at the power-of-two side carries no content of its own; `exists_wDelta2_iff` says some `ℓ₀` gives `WDelta2` iff `RunSeed N` holds, with `wDelta2_of_runSeed` and `runSeed_of_wDelta2` (sig).
  - `RelativeDecay`: the gap's decay measured against the reading rather than in the supremum norm, and where a torus needs more than that; the vacuum sandwich of the chain decay is already relative. Open: the torus-side relative form named in its text.
  - `ConstantPhysics`: the line of constant physics, the spacing read off the lattice mass `latMass D = sSup (rateSet D)`; `ConstantPhysicsE` and `StateShiftInvisibleSep` (with `stateShiftInvisible_self`) state that the signal does not see a shift of the coupling sequence. Open: `SignalConvergesSep`, `ConstantPhysicsE`.
  - `FloorFold`: the two open floors (`ReadPos.ReflFloorAt` and `RegNontrivial.regConnected_iff_floor`) compared as amplitude and slope; a reflected read is antitone, `windowUpper_of_macroReads` derives the window upper half from `MacroReadsAt`, `amplitude_of_conv` reads the amplitude from separated convergence. Open: `MacroReadsAt`.
  - `ScaleNorm`: the smeared field's two-cell-volume factor `a⁸`, computed from the smearing; `physBand_iff_ratio` shows `FloorFold.PhysBand` needs no scale as data, `slope_of_ratio`.
  - `ZoomForest`: the observer's law at every zoom as a pushforward forest; `observerFisher`, `gibbsFamily`, and the named propositions `GibbsObserverSpeed`, `DeafToUV` (the observer is deaf to the ultraviolet when its Fisher information integrates). Open: those propositions.
  - `ChiForest`: the same forest in `χ²` with variance-relative increments; it discharges the entropy propositions of `ZoomForest` (`pinskerObs`, `klDataProcessing`) from `EntropyTools`, and `ChiForest.abs_step_le` bounds a step of a mean, `abs_conn_sub_le_rel` a connected difference.
  - `Beacon`: a law is fixed by the signal a separating family of probes receives; `SchwingerBeacon` and `SchwingerBeaconSep` are the named statements, `schwingerBeaconSep_of_schwingerBeacon` takes the first to the second (sig). Open: `SchwingerBeacon`.
  - `CountDoubling`: the Rényi-2 count `R₂(m) = e(m)²/e(2m)` of the excited levels of a positive spectrum does not grow when the time doubles (`count2_double_le`, `count2_iter_le`, `excited_count_double_le`); the next-window floor is the same statement as a count bound (`wsum_double_eq`, `floor_of_count`, `count_of_floor`), and the count read at the first time bounds the rate at every later doubling (`rate_ge`, `rate_le`, `rate_le_seed`). On a finite family of levels; open: the count bound itself on the Wilson torus.
  - `LevelRate`: `BasinGrowth.RelLip` from two reads, (L) `LevelLip`, a per-level relative rate `|∂_β g_i| ≤ Λ g_i` (the lattice beta function restated level by level), and (Cnt) `CountRead`, a Shannon count of the excited levels at the window time (`axis_relLip`, `window_relLip`, `window_of_levels`, with `K = 120 Λ (1 + C)`; the exact derivative `hasDerivAt_pdef` and the split `ent_eq_shannon`). On a finite family of levels; open: (L) and (Cnt) for `SU(N)`.
  - `CentreOdds`: the two-axis inequality `AxisSplit.TransverseDouble` split over the centre sectors into a zero-flux part and the flux odds (`mixed_of_split`, `transverseDouble_of_split`, `log_hs_split`, `odds_conv_le_ratio`); the flux-tube law makes the odds part nonnegative (`oddsMixed_nonneg_of_tube`, `oddsMixed_nonneg_of_tube_sq`), Coulomb odds break it (`coulomb_odds_neg`, `confined_odds_nonneg`, `floor_needed`). Open: the zero-flux inequality and the tube law, that is confinement.
  - `OneLoopPrefactor`: (S) from the one-loop comparison with any prefactor `K β` uniform in the window and an additive slack that vanishes along the windows, from one vanishing read (`OneLoopAboveSlack`, `oneLoopAboveSlack_of_oneLoopAbove`, `strongEnd_of_oneLoopAboveSlack`, `strongEnd_of_oneLoopAboveSlack_low`); Open: the comparison `OneLoopAboveSlack` for the Wilson measure.
  - `SmallFieldStep`: one block-spin step for a linear curl and block map: the Gaussian step exactly (the fluctuation integral is independent of the block field, `fibre_gauss`, `fluct_split`), the `U(1)` small-field sandwich with `sfConst = 5/48` (`smallField_step`, `fibre_smallField`), and the window defect of an extensive or constant remainder (`defK_bulk`, `defK_const`). At quadratic order the Gaussian density keeps the same `β`; the one-loop shift needs the non-abelian cubic and quartic vertices, not built. Open: `LargeFieldSuppressed`, and `OneStepLocal`, which restates the two-sided comparison (`oneStepLocal_iff`).

## Cluster 2. The anisotropic torus and the strip

Purpose. Embed the isotropic window defect in a two-coupling family, in-plane `β` and mixed `t`. At `t = 0` the torus is exactly `2n²` independent two-dimensional tori, and the two-dimensional defect vanishes at every `β` without a character expansion. The basin squaring spreads vanishing to a strip in `t` of explicit width, and the strip carries a size-uniform transfer gap. The strip stops at `t` of order `1/β` and does not reach the diagonal `t = β`; nothing consumes it toward `StrongEnd` (paper section 7).

Capstones.

- `DecoupledSplit.ZA_decoupled`: `Z(β, 0, n) = Z₂(β, n)^{2n²}` (sig).
- `TwoDVanish.twoD_vanishes`: the two-dimensional defect tends to `0` at every `β ≥ 0`, every `N ≠ 0` (sig). `TwoDVanish.decoupled_vanishes`, `TwoDVanish.weakMixAnchor` (sig).
- `MixAnchor.DA_succ_le`, `MixAnchor.tendsto_DA_of_seed`: the basin on the anisotropic family (sig).
- `StripWidth.strip_width`: vanishing for `0 ≤ t < t_low(β) = e^{−4β}/(16192(16β+27))`, uniform in `N` (sig). `StripWidth.strip_width_poly`, `StripWiden.strip_width_glue` (sig).
- `CumulantStrip.logZA_ext`, `CumulantStrip.DA_le_quartic`, `CumulantStrip.strip_width_quartic`: the quartic cost of the mixed coupling and the widest proved strip (sig).
- `CumulantStrip.dependency_cgf`: cumulant generating function bound on a dependency graph (sig).
- `StripGap.walk_gap`: gap of the plaquette walk (sig). `StripGapThm.strip_gap`, `StripGapThm.strip_cluster`: size-uniform transfer gap on the strip, on cubic tori of side `2^w`, `w ≥ w₀(β)` (sig).
- `StripLift.gap_on_vanishSet`, `StripLift.diag_gap`, `CumulantStrip.gap_quartic` (sig).
- `MixPlane.exists_open_vanishSet`, `MixPlane.isClosed_stallSet`: the vanishing set is open and the stall set closed (sig). `MixPlane.strongEnd_iff_diag` restates `StrongEnd` on the diagonal (sig).
- `StripGas.gasR_matchAt`, `StripOsc.abs_yS_le`, `StripNest.multi_forget`: the site gas of the mixed plaquettes, translation-covariant local terms, and independence of separated rectangles, the layer of a constant-width strip that is proved (sig).
- `TwoDVanish.strongEnd_of_aniso`: `StrongEnd` from `AnisoAnalytic` frequently in `β` (sig).

Open hypotheses. `AnisoTorus.AnisoAnalytic N β` (a holomorphic extension of the defect in `t`, uniformly locally bounded on a preconnected open set; expected to fail at `U(1)`); the inequality that would close the constant-width strip (stated in the `StripTail` module text, not a declaration); `MixCutTest.VarGap`; `MixPlane.NoStallOnSegment`. None is used by the strip theorems.

Modules. `BetaAnalytic`, `LayerSplit`, `AnisoTorus`, `DecoupledSplit`, `MixAnchor`, `MixPlane`, `MixCut`, `MixCutTest`, `MixEqui`, `TwoDVanish`, `MixExpand`, `PageCouple`, `StripGap`, `StripRow`, `StripWidth`, `StripWiden`, `StripGapThm`, `StripLift`, `CumulantStrip`, `StripTail`, `StripGas`, `StripOsc`, `StripNest`, `StripClose`, `U1Strip`.

## Cluster 3. The finite-box route

Purpose. A second route to the transfer gap: a Knabe-type criterion turns a local gap on a finite box into a global gap of the heat-bath dynamics, a Lieb–Robinson bound turns that into clustering, and clustering gives the transfer gap of the periodic state. The box local gap is proved at side 15 for `SU(2)` and `SU(3)` at small coupling with explicit constants. The continuum conclusion needs an ultraviolet step that stays open; the one-cube reduction relates the route to the work of Bhattacharjee et al. (paper section 8).

Capstones.

- `KnabeCriterion.knabe_bound` (file `KnabeEngine.lean`): the algebra of Knabe's argument on any module with a non-negative form (sig).
- `HeatBathGapDecay.lr_bound`, `HeatBathGapDecay.cov_bound_exp`: a Lieb–Robinson-type bound for the lazy heat bath, free of the volume, and the covariance decay it gives (sig).
- `HeatBathLocal.irGapAt_boxRate`, `WeightedBox.irGapAt_weightedBox`: the gap of the periodic state from a box local gap (sig).
- `BoxGapBE.boxLocalGap15_su2`, `BoxGapBE.boxLocalGap15_su3`: box local gaps at side 15, uniform in the boundary (sig). `BoxGapHS.boxLocalGap15_su2_hs` (`0 ≤ β ≤ 83/1000`), `BoxGapHS.boxLocalGap15_su3_hs` (`0 ≤ β ≤ 19/100`), by `BoxGapHS.holleyStroock` (sig).
- `BoxGapBE.clay15_of_localGap`, `WeightedProfile15.clay15`, `BoxGapBE.clay_of_uvStep_su2`, `BoxGapBE.clay_of_uvStep_su3`: `ClayRoutes.ClayContinuum` from the box gap, the ultraviolet step and E, N, Y (sig).
- `UVStepMap.exists_uvBelowIR_iff_branchGap`: the ultraviolet step at some rate iff a lattice gap at one positive physical rate along the whole branch (sig). `UVStepMap.clay_of_boundedSeed` (sig).
- `OneCubeBridge.gapAt_of_cube`, `OneCubeBridge.branchGap_of_cube`, `OneCubeBridge.clay_of_cube`: a Dobrushin–Shlosman box hypothesis at a fixed physical size gives the branch gap (sig).
- `DSBoxFloor.clay_continuum_of_DSBoxRun`, `DSGaugeQuotient.clay_continuum_of_DSBoxRunQ`, `BlockGapDef.clay_continuum_of_wilsonBlockDynamics`: the same conclusion from block-dynamics hypotheses.

Open hypotheses. `ClayRoutes.UVBelowIR` (file `RestoreShimsClay.lean`) at the chosen `β₀` (the gap-side input left for `SU(2)`, `SU(3)`); `UVStepMap.BranchGap`; `UVStepMap.BoundedSeed`; `OneCubeBridge.CubeCond`, `OneCubeBridge.CubeAlong` at every `β > 0`; E `ContinuumSep.UniformBoundSep` (proved only at bounded `Z`); N `ContinuumNontrivial.KernelConvergesSep`; Y `ShortDistanceY.AFShortDistance`. `BoxGap.BoxFibreGap` is not proved at any `β > 0`; the route reads only the weaker `BoxPatch.BoxLocalGap`, which is proved. General `N` is open above the strong-coupling base. Both values of `β₀` used lie on the strong-coupling side of the crossover.

Modules.

- Knabe and heat bath: `KnabeEngine`, `KnabeWindow`, `KnabeCriterion`, `HeatBathGapDecay`, `HeatBathLocal`, `HeatBath`, `HeatBathErgodic`.
- Box patches and weights: `LocalGauge`, `UVIRSplit`, `BoxPatch`, `BoxGap`, `RestoreShims`, `RestoreShimsClay`, `WeightedKnabe`, `WeightedBridge`, `WeightedBox`, `WeightedProduct`, `WeightedProfile15`, `TwistedTransferDoubling`, `TrivialSectorDefect`.
- Pair route and Haar constants: `PairInterface`, `SimpleGroup`, `ContactValue`, `PairCount`, `PairCorr`, `PairWeight`, `HaarMomentsSU2`, `HaarMomentsSU3`, `TiltedHaar`, `PairFibreAbstract`, `PairCorrSharp`, `BoxGapBE`, `BoxGapHS`, `UVStepMap`, `BoxLatticeGap`.
- The Dobrushin–Shlosman block, from `PairFibreDens` to `OneCubeBridge`: 62 modules, lines 315 to 376 of `MassGap.lean`, among them `DSBoxFloor`, `DSGaugeQuotient`, `BlockGapDef`, `BlockDynamicStep`, `DSOllivier`, `DobrushinGeneral`, `DobrushinUnbounded`, `DobrushinW1`, `ChebyshevFilter`, `TileGeometry`, `LightCone`, `Detectability`.
- Further modules of this cluster, one line each.
  - `ScaleStep`: "zoom only coarsens" as a theorem on a view; `ViewLossStep`, `ViewLossStepBelow`, and `uvBelowIR_of_view` give `UVBelowIR` from the view. Open: the view loss step.
  - `ScaleAlong`: the ultraviolet step in the units of a scale `s` of the family instead of `aRun N`; `UVBelowIRAlong`, `irGapAlong_of_irGapAt`, `uvBelowIRAlong_of_artifact_view`. Open: `ScaleDominatesRun`.
  - `EdgeStep`: the ultraviolet step on the physical correlation length at any finite budget; `EdgeStepAbove`, `UVEdgeStepAbove`, `rate_loss_le`. Open: `EdgeStepAbove`.
  - `FibrePerturb`: the fibre covariance bound survives a bounded perturbation of the weight by `e^V` (`weight_bounds`, `iint_sep`). No open hypothesis.
  - `WeightedHeatBath`: the heat bath of an arbitrary positive continuous weight, with the adjacent-plaquette weight `wtAdj`; `fibreHyp_adj` carries `FibreHyp` to it. `WeightedTorus`: Gibbs expectation and detailed balance for a general `TorusWeight`.
  - `AdjacentFibre`: the adjacent-coupled pair weight is a tilted bilinear exponential times a small factor; counting bounds `sum_cross_le`, `sum_cross_le'` (`2304`) and `card_vis_pair_le` (`144`).
  - `ObserverBox`: the box local gap of Wilson plus an adjacent-plaquette coupling, `obsBox_localGap` for `SU(2)` at `β ≤ 1/200`, `|λ| ≤ 1/20000`, gap `593/1000`, from `defectMatrix_obs`.
  - `BoxDobrushin`: a Dobrushin influence bound for the single-link kernel (propagation, tilt bound, numbers); `oscillation_fails_sharp` shows the sharp oscillation row exceeds `1`. `BoxDobrushinCoupling`: supplies its inputs for the weight `wtV` (the pointwise ratio lemma, `sweepC`, `gibbsState`). `BoxDobrushinSpatial`: the spatial form, covariance decay with the influence distance.
  - `DobrushinMixed`: Dobrushin's theorem with a cost per site, total-variation and Kantorovich sites together (`condCovD_of_diam`). `DSBlockContract`: the block dynamics over all translates contracts the summed link metric. `CoarseDS`: the weighted Dobrushin–Shlosman box criterion gives spatial covariance decay.
  - `BlockHeatBath`: the block conditional expectations of the periodic Wilson measure; `wilsonBlockBath` builds the `BlockGapDef.WilsonBlockBath` instance and `blockGapSet_nonempty`. `BlockCommute`: locality and commutation of block expectations off shared plaquettes.
  - `OverlapGeometry`: an 81-colour cover of every periodic lattice by overlapping boxes (`card_oadj_le`, adjacency at most `624`). `SweepFilter`: covariance decay at the square root of a sweep gap, with the exact vacuum projection (`sweep_contract`). `TileSweep`: the conversion from the tiling block gap to the transfer gap with `c₀ = 1/15552` (`tileConversion`). `OverlapSweep`: the same conversion for the overlapping gap and the tower with a linear loss (`OverlapBlockStep`, `linBudget`). `OverlapFloor`: the overlapping gap as a floor at the two-loop scale. Open: `OverlapBlockStep`.
  - `OneCubeShims`: names the Dobrushin–Shlosman chain reads from the live tree (`limConn`, `RelRemainder`, `ClassKappa`, `LimitFactor`), imported once by `ObserverGapSplit`.

## Cluster 4. The statement, the observer and the continuum construction

Purpose. The target statement itself; the flowed observer through which the lattice fields are read; the Osterwalder–Schrader construction for that observer; and the existence side of the target, as capstones with named input ledgers. This is the largest cluster (about 250 modules) and the existence half of the argument (paper sections 2 and 4).

### 4a. The statement

- `ClayStatementG.ClayStatementOf G`, `ClayStatementG.ClayStatementAtP`, `ClayStatementG.ClayDataP`, `ClayStatement` (sig).
- `ClayStatement.ClayStatementAt`, `ClayStatement.ClayStatementDy`: the same clauses at the two-loop tower `dyBeta`.
- Non-vacuity: `ClayStatement.not_trace_zero`, `ClayStatement.not_mix_zero`, `ClayStatement.not_freeScalingForm`, `ClayStatement.kernel_ne_freeMassless`, `ObserverCarries.not_clayStatementAt_flatFlow`, `ObserverCarries.not_clayStatementAt_flatFlow_symPlaq`, `ObserverCarries.not_clayData_of_timeIndep` (sig; all seven are stated at the two-loop tower, `ClayStatement.ClayData`, not at the schedule along a measured tower). `ConstantPhysicsLine.not_measTowerG_const`: constant couplings are not a measured tower (sig).
- Wiring: `ClayStatement.clayStatementAt_of_link`, `ClayWiring.clayStatementAt_of_inputs`, `ClayWiringG.clayStatementAtG_of_inputsG`.

Modules: `ClayStatement`, `ClayWiring`, `ClayStatementG`, `ClayTreeG`, `MeasuredTower`, `ClayWiringG`, `FlowUV`, `GapL2`.

### 4b. Capstones over `SU(N)` and `G` with named input ledgers

Capstones, each conditional on a ledger of named inputs, later ones with shorter ledgers: `ClaySU.clayStatementAt_SU`, `ClaySUBest.clayStatementAt_SU_best`, `ClaySUFinal.clayStatementAt_SU_final`, `CompositesRenAll.clayStatementAt_SU_ren_low`, `PreObserver.clayStatementAt_SU_noNT`, `ClayRunSeed.clayStatement_of_narrowAtRS`; over `G`, `ClayG.clayStatementAtG_GS`, `ClayGFinal.clayStatementAtG_GS_noNT` and its instances at `SU`, `SO`, `SO(3)`, `USp`, `G₂`, `F₄`, `E₆`, `E₇`, `E₈`.

Reductions of single inputs that stand on their own:

- `ProcesiSeparation.tracesSeparateN_all`: traces separate points at every `N ≠ 0`, so generation on loop words is unconditional (sig).

- `ThreePointAnomaly`: the trace-anomaly identity `d/dβ cum₂ = −Σ_p cum₃(φ_p)` at finite volume.

- `StrongKernelNear`: `KernelNear` at strong coupling `|β| ≤ 2^{−16}`, by a sequential Dobrushin sweep on the Wilson specification.
- `RunSeedLink.runSeed_iff_vortex`: `RunSeed 2` iff vortex free energies at most `1/720000` and zero-flux defects at most `1/80000`, eventually in `β`.
- `ObserverTheory`: an edge cost per physical length above a cutoff iff `RunSeedG`, every `G`.

### 4c. The gap input from reads

Purpose: derive the gap input of the 4b capstones from finite-volume reads of the Wilson torus.

## Cluster 5. Separators and no-go results

Purpose. Results on what does not separate `SU(N)` from `U(1)`: each reads a property of the group or of the measure and shows that it is shared by `U(1)`, or that it leaves (S) undecided. Several are about defined models, not the Wilson measure; the paper's section 9 and `research/paper/v3/09-separators.lean-map.md` mark which.

Capstones.

- `StallRigid.fork_quant`, `StallRigid.stall_dichotomy`, `StallRigid.vanishes_iff_read`: the fork (H)/(T)/(M) for every compact group (sig). `StallRigid.schur_gap`, `StallRigid.biased_stall` (model) (sig).
- `PhotonStall.photon_not_strongEnd`, `PhotonStall.massless_vanishes_unique`: a Gaussian model with `D(β, w) = A(w) + c log β` vanishes at at most one `β` (model; the `det′` asymptotics are a hypothesis) (sig).
- `ToronSector.su2_not_strongEnd`: the one-loop toron model (sig).
- `PlaneFlow.diag_not_strip`, `PlaneFlow.window_step_id`: tree-level blocking on the coupling plane is the identity in four dimensions (a defined bookkeeping rule) (sig for `diag_not_strip`).
- `BondMove.strongEnd_of_matchedBlocking`; `OneStepZoom.stack_not_zoomStepMap` (sig): Migdal–Kadanoff-type sandwiches and the heat-kernel zoom.
- `CurveFloor.su_floor`, `CurveFloor.u1_no_floor`: the group curvature floor in the chain model (sig). `MasslessExclude.cd_fails`, `MasslessExclude.su2_class_hess`: the curvature-dimension condition fails on a gauge-invariant direction at `SU(2)` (sig for `cd_fails`).
- `CoverCompact.conserved_iff_affine`, `CoverCompact.su_everyCommutator`, `CoverCompact.conserved_const_G2`: a character neither gives nor excludes a stall; every element of `SU(N)` is a commutator (sig). `CubeFlux.bianchi_transported` (sig).
- `CoverCompact.twistedAction_flat_SU`: the centre twist at zero Wilson action at every `SU(N)` (sig). `FlatCap.flat_cap`: `−45 log N ≤ flatW ≤ 90 log N`.
- `VortexEntropy.closed_surfaces_through_plaquette` (sig): `3^k` cube-boundary surfaces of area `4k + 6` through a plaquette.
- `ThickSheetCost.uniform_trial_not_summable`, `NonCartanTwist.su2_u1_same_floor`, `FluctSheet.su2_centre_entropicShift` (sig): no classical sheet cost separates `SU(2)` from `U(1)` at fixed coupling.
- `GaugeStiffness.ward_profile`, `GaugeStiffness.resid_su2_ne` (sig): the exact stiffness Ward identity; the `SU(2)` commutator remainder is non-zero.
- `HarmonicVertex.cubic_eq_commutator`, `ExactIneq.cross_zero`, `GapPersist.zoomLimit_kinks_countable`, `DefectBound.bounded_iff_count`, `MasslessExclude.not_antitone_SU` (sig for all but `GapPersist`, `DefectBound`).

Open or chosen inputs. The perfectness of `G₂` is an instance hypothesis of `conserved_const_G2`. The model inputs (zero-mode volumes, cutoffs, the heat-kernel constant) are chosen and stated. The general compact connected case of the curvature argument, "no exact identity passes", and the absence of a four-dimensional Cramér–Rao gain are argued in module text.

Modules. `BetaFlow`, `ZoomStep`, `BondMove`, `CubeFlux`, `CurveFloor`, `PhotonStall`, `ToronSector`, `PlaneFlow`, `VortexEntropy`, `VortexSheetFlux`, `ThickSheetCost`, `NonCartanTwist`, `FluctSheet`, `OneStepZoom`, `OneLoopInput`, `HarmonicVertex`, `ExactIneq`, `CoverCompact`, `StallRigid`, `DefectBound`, `GapPersist`, `MasslessExclude`, `GaugeStiffness`, `AxisSplit`, `TransverseTunnel`.

## Cluster 6. Compact simple groups and Haar moments

Purpose. Port the doubling machinery and the statement to every compact simple gauge group, construct the exceptional groups, and supply the Haar constants the box route uses.

Capstones.

- `RectTransferG.ZRG_eq_traceSeq`, `DoublingWilsonG.factsG`, `DoublingWilsonG.factRepG`: the trace identity and trace facts for every compact `G` (sig). `TorusDoubling.defectWG_four_eq`: the defect for every compact group and dimension.

- `AbelianGen.abelianGenG_SU` (sig) and the instances in `AbelianGen`, `AbelianGenX`, `AbelianGenE7`; `ClayGFinal.clayStatementAtG_GS_noNT` at the nine groups.
- `ShiftInvG`, `AxisInvG`, `ReflInvG`: invariances of the periodic state over every `G`. `ReflPosG`: reflection positivity for even planes. `TimeGroupG.flowN_time_group_of_gapG`.
- Haar: `HaarMomentsSU2.haarConsts_su2`, `HaarVariance.haar_variance_reTr_pos` (`0 < Var(Re tr U)` at every `N ≥ 2`), `HaarMoments.haar_su2_coeff_zero`, `PlaneVariance.varReTr_pos`.

Open. The transfer gap stays an input over `G` (the gap chain is for `SU(N)` only); reflection positivity for odd planes is not ported.

## Cluster 7. Infrastructure

Purpose. The lattice gauge theory itself: Haar measure on compact groups, the Wilson action and measure, reflection positivity, transfer operators, infinite-volume Gibbs states, Gelfand–Naimark–Segal Hilbert spaces, and the strong-coupling cluster expansion.

Capstones.

- `CompactGauge.probHaar`: the Haar probability measure on a compact Hausdorff Borel group. `LatticeGauge.Symmetry.expect_invariant`, `SUN.su_expect_invariant`, `WilsonLattice.wilson_expect_invariant`.
- `ReflectionStrong.wilson_expect_nonneg_module` (sig): reflection positivity on the Wilson Gibbs state at every real `β`, for bounded measurable slab observables. `CharacterExpansion.wilson_kernel_nonneg`: `exp(β Re tr(A B†))` is positive definite for `β ≥ 0`, without representation theory.
- `WilsonDLR.exists_wilson_infinite_volume_gibbs_measure` (sig): an infinite-volume Gibbs measure for Wilson `SU(N)` at every `N ≠ 0` and every real `β`. `DLRLimit.exists_infinite_volume_gibbs_measure`, `GibbsSpec.dlr_consistent`, `PeriodicStateDLR.periodicState_dlr`.
- `StrongCoupling.boltz_eq_subset_sum`, `StrongCoupling.corrNum_eq_subset_sum`: the cluster expansion as an exact finite identity. `StrongCouplingGap.coreRate_pos`, `PeriodicStrongCoupling.torusState_connected_abs_le_of_cubes`.
- `TransferAssembly.assembleTransferData`, `SchwarzIteration`, `GNSHilbert` (a complete Hilbert space and unit vacuum from a reflection form), `ClayCapstone.clay_gap_of_gapAt`, `TransferGap.GapAt`.
- Positivity: `GibbsPositive.corrNum_plaqObs_pos`, `PlaqVariance.corrClay_zero_pos`, `ContactFloor.wilsonCorrConn_self_ge_haar`, `ChessboardRead.lagProfile_log_convex`.
- `Reflect`, `OddLagSplit`, `CrossingIntegration`: reflection positivity at odd lag, refuted in the cheap form (`straddling_word_not_matrix_paired_at_odd_lag`) and proved another way.

Modules. `WilsonHypercubic`, `WilsonBridge`, `LatticeGauge`, `CompactGauge`, `SUN`, `WilsonAction`, `WilsonLattice`, `WilsonReal`, `ReflectionPositivity`, `Reflect`, `Transfer`, `WilsonAnalytic`, `StrongCoupling`, `OddLagSplit`, `CrossingIntegration`, `CharacterExpansion`, `ActionSplit`, `GibbsPositive`, `PlaqVariance`, `ReflectPositive`, `LogConvex`, `ContactFloor`, `ReflectionStrong`, `WilsonTransfer`, `HalfLineTransfer`, `InfiniteLattice`, `InfiniteShift`, `LatticeReflection`, `ReflectionShift`, `HalfSpaceAlgebra`, `SchwarzIteration`, `TransferAssembly`, `WilsonTransferReduction`, `WilsonState`, `GibbsSpec`, `DLRLimit`, `InfiniteReflection`, `OSPositivity`, `WilsonDLR`, `SliceTrace`, `SecondEigenvalue`, `LinkGram`, `GNSHilbert`, `GNSCompare`, `ClayCapstone`, `GaugeInvariantAlgebra`, `GapToOperator`, `SlabKernelOperator`, `SlabTransferAdjoint`, `FiniteOrderTransfer`, `AsymptoticScaling`, `TransferGap`, `StrongCouplingGap`, `GeneralDecay`, `PlaneVariance`, `PeriodicState`, `PeriodicReduce`, `PeriodicContent`, `ChessboardRead`.

## Cluster 8. Other routes

Purpose. Routes the paper does not follow: one bridge on the Wilson box, a Bałaban-type ultraviolet hypothesis, Migdal–Kadanoff and Tomboulis–Yaffe sandwiches, vortex condensation. They are conditional on hypotheses that are open or, in some cases, false; the results that stand alone are listed in the next section.

- `OneBridge.clayStatementAt_SU_oneBridge`: the `SU(N)` capstone with the gap input as one named bridge `OneBridge` on the Wilson box.
- `TomboulisYaffe`: `TYBoundR` is false at `a = b` and stronger than the published bound; the centre flip, sheet translation and crossing are proved. `VortexTwo`: its hypotheses are jointly false at some `β`. `MKBound`: a Tomboulis-type sandwich, all but `SandwichMatch` proved. `BalabanUV.BalabanShape`: a Bałaban-type hypothesis, not his theorem. `FluxTube`: false classically for `SU(N)`, true for finite groups. `CrossTerm`: `QFloor` is false.

Open hypotheses. `OneBridge`, `CondGap.WilsonCondUVGap`, `MatchAt`, `SandwichMatch`, `BondMoveCost`.

Modules. `FineEnd`, `FixedAperture`, `RelativeGap`, `ReadsSlabWilson`, `LayerRate`, `UniformAperture`, `JentzschKT`, `SlabMeasure`, `CondGap`, `OneBridge`, `WilsonSlab`, `FineLinks`, `CellLinks`, `MatchAt`, `BalabanUV`, `NoTransitionFlow`, `MKBound`, `OneWayCost`, `FluxTube`, `VortexCondense`, `VortexTorus`, `PolymerCount`, `ChunkActivity`, `TomboulisYaffe`, `VortexTwo`, `ChunkLinks`, `CrossTerm`, `VortexStrong`, `Chessboard`.

## Off the main path, useful

Self-contained results that no theorem of the paper uses.

- Spectral measures of reflection-positive correlators: `ShellMeasure`, `ShellGap`, `ShellMomentum`, `ShellTranslate`, `ShellJoint`, `ShellDensity`, `ShellLorentz`, `BernsteinWidder`, `StieltjesSector`, `FourierSector`, `KernelRate`, `MomentumSector`, `LorentzStep`, `LorentzConverse` (a Källén–Lehmann-type spectral measure, Bernstein–Widder, Gelfand duality).
- `FreqRead`, `BandShell`, `WindowFreq`: the doubling defect of a free periodic chain vanishes exactly when the mass is positive.
- `KnabeEngine`, `KnabeWindow`: a standalone finite-size criterion for spectral gaps.
- `HaarMomentsSU2`, `HaarMomentsSU3`, `TiltedHaar`; `BoxGapHS.holleyStroock` (the Poincaré constant under bounded tilts).
- `DSOllivier`: contraction of a summed link metric gives the Poincaré inequality without a spectral theorem. `DobrushinGeneral`, `DobrushinUnbounded`, `DobrushinW1`, `ChebyshevFilter`, `TileGeometry`, `LightCone`, `Detectability`.
- `JentzschKT` (a strictly positive top eigenfunction), `WilsonSlab` (simple top eigenvalue, ratio below one), `SlabMeasure` (the Doob transform), `PolymerCount` (the Kotecký–Preiss count, a lattice-animal bound `4225^n`), `ChunkActivity`.
- `VortexStrong`: condensation of `vortexR` on `SU(2)` doubling tori at `0 < β ≤ 1/4000000`, rate at least `log 128`, by a polymer expansion and sheet invisibility. `Chessboard`: reflection positivity on `RSite`, Schwarz, doubling, `chain_full`.
- `ProcesiSeparation.tracesSeparateN_all`, `LoopsGenerate`, `ThreePointAnomaly`, `TreeCount`, `StrongKernelNear` (cluster 4b).
- `GaugeG2` to `GaugeE8` (cluster 6).
- `EntropyTools` (imports Mathlib only): data processing for pushforwards, `klDiv (P.map χ) (Q.map χ) ≤ klDiv P Q` for any measurable `χ` (`klDiv_map_le`), and Pinsker's inequality for `klDiv`, neither of which Mathlib states at the pin; elementary steps `three_mul_sq_le_klFun`, `sq_le_mul_of_forall_two_mul_le`. Used by `ChiForest` (cluster 1).
- `PurityBound`: Sections 2–4 of the 4Y-M second paper (Bhattacharjee et al., *Thermal Purity Bounds for Yang–Mills Theory at Weak Coupling*, draft of 15 November 2026). From the trace facts `DoublingPower.PowerFacts`, a purity `P = exp(−defect) > 1/2` gives the excited weight `s ≤ d(P) = (1 − √(2P − 1))/(1 + √(2P − 1))` (`excited_le_sharp`), attained by a two-level spectrum (`purity_two_level`); the two-level defect `twoLevel` is strictly increasing and concave on `[0, log 2]` with the chord bound (`twoLevel_chord`); certified intervals (`purity_mem_of_intervals`, `defect_le_of_approx`); the window conversion (`pressure_le`), `1/161 ≤ twoLevel (1/320)` (`inv161_le_twoLevel`) and the datum `defect ≤ V/(161 W) ⇒ log(1 + s) ≤ V/(320 W)` (`window_datum`). `d_antitoneOn`, `excited_le_of_purity_ge`: a certified purity bound `P ≥ p > 1/2` gives `s ≤ d(p)`. `wilson_facts`, `wilson_excited_le_sharp`, `wilson_window_datum`: the same for the `L²` operator `wilsonK` of the transverse transfer kernel of the Wilson model of every gauge group, with `Tr K^{2j} = ‖K‖^{2j}(1 + s)` at every even time `2j`, where `defect` is the time-doubling pressure defect at fixed spatial torus.
- `PurityGap`: Theorem 2.2, (2.3), of the same paper as an operator gap: for a bounded symmetric kernel operator with a normalized top eigenfunction, a pressure defect at time `2j` below `log 2` gives `‖K^j f‖ ≤ √(d(exp(−defect))) ‖K‖^j ‖f‖` on the vacuum complement (`norm_pow_le_sharp`).
- `PurityStability`: Propositions 3.1 and 3.2 of the same paper, for density operators on a finite-dimensional inner product space, with the trace norm of a symmetric operator as the sum of the absolute values of its eigenvalues (`tnorm`). `purity_diff_le`: `|Tr ρ² − Tr σ²| ≤ ‖ρ − σ‖₁`. `truncation_le`: for a symmetric idempotent `P` with `q = re Tr((1 − P) ρ) < 1`, the truncated state `(1 − q)⁻¹ P ρ P` (`trunc`) is within `2√q + q` of `ρ` in trace norm. The duality is attained by the sign operator of the difference (`sgnOp`, `re_trace_mul_sgnOp`, `norm_inner_sgnOp_le`).
- `PurityScalar` (imports Mathlib only): the scalar content of Sections 6 and 7 of the same paper. `ascent`: Lemma 7.2, `z ≥ 0`, `z₀ ≤ cq`, `z_{i+1} ≤ z_i² + e_i`, `e_i ≤ c(1 − c)q^{2^{i+1}}` give `z_i ≤ cq^{2^i}`; `ascent_wilson`: the instance `A = 1683/100`, `c = 9/10`, `q = 187/200` with reserve `3/561`, `y₀ ≤ 1/20 ⇒ y_i ≤ (10/187)(187/200)^{2^i}`; `constant_tolerance`: the stationary level `r = (1 − √(1 − 4Aη))/(2A) = Ar² + η` of a constant error. `bulk_cancel`, `bulk_cancel_pressure`, `multiscale`: Proposition 7.1, the bulk term `σVt` cancels in `2 log Z(T) − log Z(2T)` and the blocked pressures `T³ψ/V` telescope with remainders `T³/V (2R₁ − R₂)`, each at most `4εT⁴` under `|R(t)| ≤ εVt` (`separate_bounds`). `geom_purity`, `geom_defect_ge`: the geometric spectrum `(1 − q)qⁿ` has purity `(1 − q)/(1 + q)` and defect at least `2q`; `gaussian_mode_defect`: Proposition 6.3, `Tω_{2T} ≤ π` with `ω_L = 2 arsinh(sin(π/L))`, so the lowest Gaussian mode has defect above `2/81`, above the window budget `1/(161 · 13824)` (`budget_eq`). `kernel_route_large`: Proposition 6.2, with `τ = tanh(κ/2)`, `κ ≥ T³`, `T ≥ 3`, `log(τ^T/(1 − τ²)) ≥ 22` and the kernel bound `2 log(1 + U)` exceeds `44`.
- `PurityRow` (imports Mathlib only): Lemma 5.1 of the same paper. For a finite measure `w ≠ 0` and measurable `f, g` with values in `[a, b]`, `0 < a ≤ b`, `row w f E = ∫_E f dw / ∫ f dw`. `row_abs_le`: (5.1), `|row w f E − row w g E| ≤ (b − a)/(b + a)` for every measurable `E`, which is the total-variation bound in its sup-over-sets form; `row_le` is the one-sided form; `row_sharp`: equality for the two-valued densities `b, a` and `a, b` on a set of half the mass; `row_sharp_bool`: the paper's witness, two points of equal mass (`Bool`, counting measure, `E = {true}`). `avg_abs_le`: the bounded-function form used in the proof of Theorem 5.2, `|∫h dP − ∫h dQ| ≤ (b − a)/(b + a) · (M − m)` for measurable `h` with values in `[m, M]`, where `avg w f h = ∫ h f dw / ∫ f dw`; `avg_sub_le` is the one-sided form, proved by `∫ h D = ∫ (h − m) D ≤ (M − m)(P(E) − Q(E))` with `D = f/∫f − g/∫g`, `E = {D > 0}`, and `row_le`. The proof bounds the integrals over `E` and `Eᶜ` by `[a w(E), b w(E)]` and `[a w(Eᶜ), b w(Eᶜ)]` (`setIntegral_mem`, `frac_le_corner`, `corner_le_frac`) and closes with `corner_le`, `(b − a)(xy(a + b)² − (bx + ay)(ax + by)) = −ab(b − a)(x − y)²`, in place of the paper's likelihood-ratio chord argument.
- `PurityContraction` (imports `PurityRow`): the contraction step of Theorem 5.2 of the same paper. The kernel is `K(x, y) = D(x) A(x, y) D(y)` on a finite measure `μ ≠ 0`, with `D` and the top eigenfunction `h` measurable and bounded above and below by positive constants, `A(x, ·)` measurable with `0 < a ≤ A ≤ b`, and `∫ K(x, y) h(y) dμ(y) = λ₀ h(x)`, `λ₀ > 0`. `doob_row`: for an eigenfunction `ψ`, `(λ/λ₀)(ψ/h)(x)` is the average of `ψ/h` against the density `A(x, ·)` with respect to `dw = D h dμ`, the factor `D(x)` cancelling. `weighted_abs_le`: `avg_abs_le` for `ρ dμ`, through `withDensity`. `eigen_le`: (5.2), `|λ/λ₀| ≤ (b − a)/(b + a)` for every eigenfunction with `ψ/h` bounded and nonconstant, by bounding `|(λ/λ₀)(φ(x) − φ(x'))|`, `φ = ψ/h`, by `(b − a)/(b + a) · (sup φ − inf φ)` row by row and taking the supremum over `x` and the infimum over `x'`; the bound does not involve `D`. `top_simple`: an eigenfunction at `λ₀` with `ψ/h` bounded is a multiple of `h`. Not formalized here: compactness and self-adjointness of the operator, the existence, positivity and continuity of `h`, continuity of every eigenfunction (so `ψ/h` bounded is a hypothesis), that the nonzero spectrum consists of eigenvalues, the gap `−log ρ`, and the two-point sharpness example. For `RectTransferG.kTG`, simplicity and the gap are `PurityLatticeGap.excited`; on a compact space, the positive top eigenfunction and `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖` are `PurityWilsonCompact` and `PurityThermalCompact`; the two-point family is `PurityDegenerate`.
- `PurityWilson` (imports `PurityContraction`): The analytic steps of Lemma 5.3 (5.5) and Theorem 5.4 (5.6) of the same paper. `ratio_tanh`: `(b − a)/(b + a) = tanh(½ log(b/a))` for `a, b > 0`, through `tanh x = 1 − 2/(e^{2x} + 1)` (`tanh_eq_one_sub`, with `tanh_mono`, `tanh_lt_one`, `tanh_pos`, `neg_log_tanh_pos`: `−log tanh x > 0` for `x > 0`). `kernel_lower`: for a probability measure `ν` and integrable `F` with `e^F` integrable, `∫ F dν = c` gives `e^c ≤ ∫ e^F dν` (Jensen, `ConvexOn.map_integral_le`); `kernel_upper`: `F ≤ c` gives `∫ e^F dν ≤ e^c`. `wilson_ratio`: with `a = e^{βMq/d}`, `b = e^{βM}`, `M = 3L³`, `(b − a)/(b + a) = tanh(3βL³(1 − q/d)/2)`; `wilson_ratio_le`: (5.6), this is at most `tanh(3βL³/2) < 1` for `β, L, q ≥ 0`, `d > 0`; `wilson_gap_pos`: `−log tanh(3βL³/2) > 0` for `β > 0`, `L ≥ 1`. `wilson_eigen_le`: (5.6) on the hypotheses of `PurityContraction.eigen_le`, with the pointwise bounds `e^{3βL³q/d} ≤ A ≤ e^{3βL³}` as hypotheses and `0 ≤ q ≤ d`: `|λ/λ₀| ≤ tanh(3βL³(1 − q/d)/2) ≤ tanh(3βL³/2)` for every eigenfunction with `ψ/h` bounded and nonconstant. The concrete Wilson transfer kernel is `RectTransferG.kTG` (`RectTransfer.kT` for SU(N)), composed with these bounds in `PurityRectWilson`. The Haar average is `PurityHaarProjection.integral_wilson_edge`, `Re Tr W ≤ d` is `GaugeGroup.abs_re_trace_repM_le`. Not formalized: simplicity of `λ₀` as a stated theorem (for `RectTransferG.kTG` it is `PurityLatticeGap.excited`); positivity and injectivity of the operator, including `λ₁ > 0` (for `RectTransferG.kTG`, `K ≥ 0` is `PurityKernelPositive.kTG_quad_nonneg` and `λ₁ > 0` is `PurityLam1Pos.lam1_pos`), and compactness as a stated property (only its consequence, the norm attained at an eigenfunction, `KernelEigen.exists_eigen`, is proved); the step from the eigenvalue ratio bound to `Δ_lat ≥ −log tanh(3βL³/2)` (for `RectTransferG.kTG` it is `PurityLatticeGap.excited` with `PurityLatticeGap.gap_uniform`); gauge invariance of the top eigenfunction; and the rank-one kernel at `β = 0`. On a compact space, the composition of the kernel bounds with Theorem 5.2, the existence of the positive top eigenfunction and `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖` are `PurityWilsonCompact`.
- `PurityLaplace` (Mathlib only): Proposition A.1 of the same paper. For a probability measure `μ` and `0 ≤ g ≤ 1` with `g ≤ ε` off a measurable `S` and `g ≥ ε` on `S`: `ε μ(S) ≤ ∫ g dμ ≤ μ(S) + ε(1 − μ(S))` (`split_ge`, `split_le`), so `c ≤ ∫ g dμ` and `ε < 1` give `μ(S) ≥ (c − ε)/(1 − ε)` (`split_rearrange`). On `[0, ∞] = ℝ≥0∞` with `lap t E = e^{−tE}` and `e^{−t∞} = 0`: `R(t) = ∫ lap t dμ ≤ 1` for `t ≥ 0` (`integral_lap_le_one`); `R(t) ≥ c > 0` at `t > 0` gives `μ([0, t⁻¹ log(2/c)]) ≥ c/(2 − c)` (`laplace_floor`), and `μ([0, Λ]) ≥ δ` gives `R(t) ≥ δ e^{−tΛ}` for `t, Λ ≥ 0` (`laplace_floor_converse`). `escape_tendsto`: the counterexample sequence `e^{−kt} → 0` for `t > 0`; `dirac_lap`, `dirac_weight_zero`: its spectral measures `δ_k` have `R_k(t) = e^{−kt}` and `δ_k([0, Λ]) = 0` for `k > Λ ≥ 0`. In finite dimension the spectral measure is `PuritySpectralMeasure`. For a bounded `0 ≤ a ≤ 1` on a complex Hilbert space, the spectral measure of `u` and `Re ⟪u, a^τ u⟫ = ∫ e^{−τE} dσ_u` at real `τ > 0` are `MassGap.ShellMeasure` (`vecMeasure`, `rateMeasure`, `re_inner_powT_eq_laplace`); `rateMeasure` lives on `ℝ` and omits the mass at `λ = 0`. The push-forward of `vecMeasure` to a probability measure on `[0, ∞]` carrying the mass at `λ = 0`, and the proposition at real `t > 0`, are `PurityShellFloor`. Not formalized: the unbounded generator `H ≥ 0`; the two-vector Hilbert-space model of the counterexample; the remark that a lower gap `m` gives the interval `[m, Λ]`.
- `PurityFiniteRank` (imports `PurityTrace`): the scalar content of Theorem D.1 of the same paper, with the operator inputs as hypotheses (`λ₀ ≥ δ = μ₀ − ε > 0`, `0 ≤ λ_j ≤ μ_j + ε` for `1 ≤ j < m`, tail values `0 ≤ ρ_k` with `Σ ρ_k² ≤ ε²`, over a finite index set or as a `tsum`). `mul_sq_le_pow`: `S ≤ ε²` gives `ε^(T−2) S ≤ ε^T`; `le_of_sq_le`: `0 ≤ x`, `x² ≤ ε²` give `x ≤ ε`. `tail_pow_le`: `Σ ρ_k^T ≤ ε^T` for `T ≥ 2`. `head_le`: `Σ_j (λ_j/λ₀)^T ≤ Σ_j ((μ_j + ε)/δ)^T`; `div_pow_le`: `X ≤ ε^T` gives `X/λ₀^T ≤ (ε/δ)^T`. `excited_le`: (D.1) for `T ≥ 2`, `Σ_j (λ_j/λ₀)^T + Σ_k (ρ_k/λ₀)^T ≤ Σ_j ((μ_j + ε)/δ)^T + (ε/δ)^T = d_T`. `tail_tsum_le`: summable `Σ' ρ_k² ≤ ε²` gives summable `ρ_k^T` with `Σ' ρ_k^T ≤ ε^T`. `excited_tsum_le`: (D.1) with the tail as a `tsum`. `pressure_le`: `0 < X ≤ 1 + d_T`, `Y ≥ 1` give `2 log X − log Y ≤ 2 log(1 + d_T)`. `ratio_lt_one`, `ratio_le_root`: nonnegative ratios with `Σ r_j^T ≤ d < 1` are each `< 1`, and each is at most `d^{1/T}`. Not formalized: operator norm ≤ Hilbert–Schmidt norm, `(I − P)B = 0` giving `‖(I − P)K‖_HS ≤ ε`; min–max, the variational bound, `Tr K² = Σ_j λ_j²` and the spectral expansion of `Tr R^T` for a trace-class `K` on an infinite-dimensional space; simplicity of the top eigenvalue from ratios `< 1`. In finite dimension the variational bound `Tr(P K²) ≤ Σ_{j<m} λ_j²` is `PurityKyFan`, and `Tr K² = Σ_j λ_j²`, the spectral expansion of `Tr R^T`, `Tr R^T ≥ 1` for `K ≥ 0` and `Tr R^{2T} ≥ 1` are `PuritySpectralTrace`, and min–max `λ_j ≤ μ_j + ε` (Weyl) is `PurityWeyl`. The matrix construction (D.2), the tail identity `‖(I − P)K‖²_HS = Tr K² − Tr(P K²)` and the window test (D.3) are `PurityTailIdentity`.
- `PurityKyFan` (imports `PurityStability`): the variational bound of Appendix D of the same paper on a finite-dimensional inner product space. `sum_mul_le_head`: weights `0 ≤ w_i ≤ 1` with `Σ w_i ≤ |h|`, and `α ≥ c ≥ 0` on `h`, `α ≤ c` off `h`, give `Σ α_i w_i ≤ Σ_{i∈h} α_i`. `diag_proj_mem`: for a symmetric idempotent `P` and a unit vector `x`, `0 ≤ re⟪x, P x⟫ ≤ 1` (from `PurityStability.norm_sq_proj`, `norm_proj_le`). `re_trace_sq_mul_eq`: `re Tr(K K P) = Σ_i λ_i² re⟪e_i, P e_i⟫` in an eigenbasis of symmetric `K`. `variational_le`: for `K ≥ 0` and an orthogonal projection `P` with `re Tr P ≤ m`, `re Tr(P K²) ≤ Σ_{i<m} λ_i²`, eigenvalues in decreasing order.
- `PuritySpectralTrace` (Mathlib only): the spectral expansion used in Proposition 6.1 and Theorem D.1 of the same paper, on a finite-dimensional inner product space, for symmetric `K` with eigenvalues `λ_i` and `R = λ_{i₀}⁻¹ K`. `pow_apply_eigenvectorBasis`: `K^k e_i = λ_i^k e_i`. `re_inner_pow_eq`: `re ⟪K^T x, x⟫ = Σ_i λ_i^T ‖⟪e_i, x⟫‖²`. `trace_pow_eq`, `re_trace_pow_eq`: `Tr K^k = Σ_i λ_i^k` (at `k = 2`, `Tr K² = Σ_i λ_i²`). `re_trace_pow_eq_of_basis`: `re Tr C^k = Σ_i μ_i^k` for any orthonormal basis with `C b_i = μ_i b_i`. `re_trace_smul_pow`: `re Tr (c K)^T = c^T re Tr K^T`. `trace_ratio_eq`: `re Tr R^T = 1 + Σ_{i ≠ i₀} (λ_i/λ_{i₀})^T` for `λ_{i₀} ≠ 0`. `one_le_trace_ratio_even`: `re Tr R^{2T} ≥ 1`. `one_le_trace_ratio`: `re Tr R^T ≥ 1` for `K ≥ 0` and `λ_{i₀} > 0`.
- `PurityWeyl` (imports `PuritySpectralTrace`): the min–max step `λ_j ≤ μ_j + ε` of Theorem D.1 of the same paper, on a finite-dimensional inner product space, for symmetric `K`, `B` with eigenvalues `λ_j`, `μ_j` sorted in decreasing order and eigenbases `e_i`, `f_i`. `re_inner_eq_sum`: `re ⟪K x, x⟫ = Σ_i λ_i ‖⟪e_i, x⟫‖²`, the case `T = 1` of `PuritySpectralTrace.re_inner_pow_eq`. `head_le`: `⟪e_i, x⟫ = 0` for `i > j` gives `λ_j ‖x‖² ≤ re ⟪K x, x⟫`. `le_tail`: `⟪f_i, x⟫ = 0` for `i < j` gives `re ⟪B x, x⟫ ≤ μ_j ‖x‖²`. `inner_eq_zero_of_mem_span`: a vector in the span of `b_k`, `k ∈ s`, is orthogonal to `b_i`, `i ∉ s`. `exists_ne_zero_inter_map` (`⟪c_i, D x⟫ = 0` for a linear map `D`), `exists_ne_zero_inter`: for orthonormal bases `b`, `c` and `j` there is `x ≠ 0` with `⟪b_i, x⟫ = 0` for `i > j` and `⟪c_i, x⟫ = 0` for `i < j`. `eigenvalues_le_mul_add`: `re ⟪K x, x⟫ ≤ c re ⟪B x, x⟫ + ε ‖x‖²` for all `x` and `c ≥ 0` give `λ_j ≤ c μ_j + ε`; `eigenvalues_le_add` is the case `c = 1`. `form_le_of_norm_sub_le`: `‖(K − B) x‖ ≤ ε ‖x‖` gives that form bound. `abs_eigenvalues_sub_le`: `‖(K − B) x‖ ≤ ε ‖x‖` for all `x` gives `|λ_j − μ_j| ≤ ε`.
- `PuritySpectralMeasure` (imports `PurityLaplace`, `PuritySpectralTrace`): the spectral measure of Proposition A.1 for a transfer operator on a finite-dimensional inner product space, as an explicit sum of Dirac masses; the infinite-dimensional spectral measure is `ShellMeasure.vecMeasure`. For symmetric `K` with eigenbasis `e_i` and energies `E(λ) = −log λ` (`∞` at `λ = 0`), `specMeasure` is `μ_v = Σ_i ‖⟪e_i, v⟫‖² δ_{E(λ_i)}` on `ℝ≥0∞`: `μ_v([0, ∞]) = ‖v‖²` (`specMeasure_univ`, `isProbability`), `μ_v(S) = Σ_{E_i ∈ S} ‖⟪e_i, v⟫‖²` (`specMeasure_real_apply`), `∫ e^{−tE} dμ_v = Σ_i ‖⟪e_i, v⟫‖² e^{−tE_i}` (`integral_lap`), `e^{−TE(λ)} = λ^T` (`lap_energy`) and `e^{−tE(λ)} = λ^t` for real `t > 0` (`lap_energy_rpow`), `E` measurable (`measurable_energy`), `E(λ) ≤ Λ ↔ e^{−Λ} ≤ λ` (`energy_le_iff`), and with `PuritySpectralTrace.re_inner_pow_eq`, for `K ≥ 0` with eigenvalues `λ_i ≤ 1`, `re ⟪K^T v, v⟫ = ∫ e^{−TE} dμ_v` for natural `T ≥ 1` (`transfer_eq_integral`). `transfer_floor`: for `‖v‖ = 1` and `re ⟪K^T v, v⟫ ≥ c > 0`, `Σ_{λ_i ≥ e^{−Λ}} ‖⟪e_i, v⟫‖² ≥ c/(2 − c)` with `Λ = T⁻¹ log(2/c)`.
- `PurityShellFloor` (imports `PuritySpectralMeasure`, `ShellMeasure`): Proposition A.1 for a self-adjoint `a` on a complex Hilbert space with spectrum in `[0, 1]`. `energyMeasure`: the spectral measure `σ_u = ShellMeasure.vecMeasure a u` pushed forward under `λ ↦ E(λ)` to `ℝ≥0∞`, with the mass at `λ = 0` at `E = ∞`; `μ_u([0, ∞]) = ‖u‖²` (`energyMeasure_real_univ`, from `ShellMeasure.vecMeasure_real_univ`), a probability measure for `‖u‖ = 1` (`isProbability`); `Re ⟪u, a^t u⟫ = ∫ e^{−tE} dμ_u` for real `t > 0` (`re_inner_powT_eq_integral`). `shell_floor`: `‖u‖ = 1`, `Re ⟪u, a^t u⟫ ≥ c > 0` give `σ_u([e^{−Λ}, 1]) ≥ c/(2 − c)` for `Λ = t⁻¹ log(2/c)`.
- `PurityFreeField` (Mathlib only): elementary scalar steps of Proposition B.1 of the same paper (generalized free field, `ρ(s) = c s²/log²(e + s)`, `c = d∗/(16π²)`). `energy_mem`: `0 < s < ε²/4`, `0 ≤ p < ε/2` give `0 < √(p² + s) < ε`. `log_split`, `quotient_tendsto_L`, `quotient_tendsto`: at fixed `u > 0`, `log²(1/r)/log²(e + u²/r²) → 1/4` as `r → 0⁺`. `poly_integral`: `∫₀¹ (1 − z²)² dz = 8/15`. `moment_value`: `6! · 8/15 = 384`. `limit_coeff`: `c/(2π²) · 384 · 1/4 = 48c/π²`. `limit_constant`: `= 3d∗/π⁴`. Not formalized: the Wightman construction, Fock space, locality and positivity; temperedness of `ρ`; the identification of the field's separated Euclidean-time covariance with the `K₁` integral `PurityFreeFieldLimit.C` (taken there as its definition); vacuum uniqueness; the divergence of the raw Euclidean momentum integral. `K₁`, its `q = sinh v` representation, `K₁(u) ≤ 1/u` and `∫₀^∞ u⁶ K₁ = 6! ∫₀^∞ sech⁶ = 384` with integrability of `u⁶ K₁` are `PurityBesselK`; integrability of the `C` integrand, the change of variables (B.1) and the limit `r⁸ log²(1/r) C(r) → 3d∗/π⁴` are `PurityFreeFieldLimit`; the positive measure of the low-energy region, `E > 0` a.e. and the no-gap conclusion are `PurityFreeFieldSpectrum`.
- `PurityBesselK` (imports `PurityWilson`, `PurityFreeField`): the modified Bessel function `K₁` of Appendix B of the same paper, defined by `K1 u = ∫₀^∞ e^{−u cosh v} cosh v dv` (DLMF 10.32.9; Mathlib has no modified Bessel functions); `K1_nonneg`; `continuous_K1_integrand`. `mul_exp_neg_le`: `x e^{−ux} ≤ (2/u) e^{−ux/2}` at `u > 0`, the domination behind `integrableOn_K1` (integrability on `[0, ∞)` at `u > 0`, with `v ≤ cosh v`). `sqrt_one_add_sinh_sq`: `√(1 + sinh² v) = cosh v`. `continuous_sqrt_integrand`; `exp_sqrt_le`: `e^{−u√(1+q²)} ≤ e^{−u|q|}` at `u ≥ 0`; `integrableOn_sqrt`. `K1_eq_sqrt`: `K₁(u) = ∫₀^∞ e^{−u√(1+q²)} dq` at `u > 0` by `q = sinh v` (`integral_comp_mul_deriv_Ioi`). `K1_le`: `K₁(u) ≤ 1/u` at `u > 0`, from `|q| ≤ √(1+q²)` and `∫₀^∞ e^{−uq} dq = 1/u`. `moment_gamma`: `∫₀^∞ u⁶ e^{−cu} du = 720/c⁷` at `c > 0` (`Γ(7) = 6!`); `moment_inner`: `∫₀^∞ u⁶ e^{−u cosh v} cosh v du = 720/cosh⁶ v`; `moment_K1_sech`: `∫₀^∞ u⁶ K₁ = 6! ∫₀^∞ sech⁶` (Tonelli, `integral_integral_swap`, integrability by `integrable_prod_iff'`). `one_sub_tanh_sq`: `1 − tanh² = sech²`; `hasDerivAt_tanh`; `tendsto_tanh_atTop` (via `PurityWilson.tanh_eq_one_sub`). `integral_sech6`: `∫₀^∞ sech⁶ = 8/15` by the antiderivative `t − 2t³/3 + t⁵/5` at `t = tanh v`; `integral_sech6_eq_poly`: `= ∫₀¹ (1 − z²)² dz` (`PurityFreeField.poly_integral`). `moment_K1`: `∫₀^∞ u⁶ K₁(u) du = 384` (`PurityFreeField.moment_value`); `integrableOn_moment_K1`: `u⁶ K₁` is integrable on `(0, ∞)`.
- `PurityFreeFieldLimit` (imports `PurityBesselK`): `ρ`, `C(r)`, the change of variables (B.1) and the `r → 0⁺` limit of Proposition B.1 of the same paper, with `L = log(1/r)` and `q r u = L²/log²(e + u²/r²)`. `one_le_log`: `1 ≤ log(e + x)` at `x ≥ 0`. `q_nonneg`, `q_le_sq`: `0 ≤ q ≤ L²`. `q_le_one`: `q ≤ 1` at `0 < r < 1`, `u > √r`. `measurable_q`; `integrableOn_moment_q`: `u⁶ K₁ q` is integrable on `(0, ∞)`. `moment_K1_le`: `u⁶ K₁(u) ≤ u⁵` at `u > 0`. `tail_le`: `∫_{(0, √r]} u⁶ K₁ q ≤ L² r³/6`; `tail_bound_tendsto`: `L² r³/6 → 0`. `head_tendsto`: `∫_{(√r, ∞)} u⁶ K₁ q → 96` (dominated convergence, bound `u⁶ K₁`, pointwise limit `u⁶ K₁/4`). `moment_limit`: `∫₀^∞ u⁶ K₁(u) L²/log²(e + u²/r²) du → 96` as `r → 0⁺`. `limit_scaled`: `c/(2π²) ∫ … → 48c/π²`; `limit_scaled_constant`: `→ 3d∗/π⁴` at `c = d∗/(16π²)`. `rho`: `ρ(s) = c s²/log²(e + s)`; `integrand`: `ρ(s) √s K₁(r√s)/(4π² r)`; `C`: `C(r) = ∫₀^∞ integrand(s) ds`. `integral_comp_sq`: `∫₀^∞ f(s) ds = ∫₀^∞ 2v f(v²) dv`. `integrand_comp`: after `s = v²`, `v = u/r`, `2v · integrand(v²) = c/(2π²) r⁻⁷ u⁶ K₁(u)/log²(e + u²/r²)`. `integrableOn_moment_log`: `u⁶ K₁(u)/log²(e + u²/r²)` is integrable on `(0, ∞)`. `integrableOn_integrand`: the integrand of `C` is integrable on `(0, ∞)` at `r > 0`. `covariance_eq`: (B.1), `r⁸ L² C(r) = c/(2π²) ∫₀^∞ u⁶ K₁ q_r` at `r > 0`. `covariance_limit`: `r⁸ log²(1/r) C(r) → 3d∗/π⁴` as `r → 0⁺` at `c = d∗/(16π²)`.
- `PurityFreeFieldSpectrum` (imports `PurityFreeField`, `PurityFreeFieldLimit`): the one-particle measure of Proposition B.1 of the same paper has no gap. `energy`: `E(s, p) = √(|p|² + s)` on `(s, p) ∈ ℝ × ℝ³`; `density`: `ρ(s)/(2E)`; `oneParticle c`: `ρ(s)/(2E) · (ds|_{(0,∞)} ⊗ d³p)`, taken as the one-particle spectral measure; `lowRegion ε`: `0 < s < ε²/4`, `|p| < ε/2`. `energy_lowRegion`: `0 < E < ε` there (`PurityFreeField.energy_mem`); `rho_pos`, `density_pos`: positivity at `c > 0`, `s > 0`; `oneParticle_lowRegion_pos`, `low_energy_pos`: `lowRegion ε` and `{0 < E < ε}` have positive measure at `c > 0`, `ε > 0`; `energy_pos_ae`: `E > 0` a.e.; `no_gap`, `no_mass_gap`: at `c > 0` no `m > 0` has `E ≥ m` a.e. Not formalized here: the Wightman construction, Fock space, locality and positivity, the identification of `oneParticle` with the field's one-particle spectral measure, and vacuum uniqueness.
- `PurityCharacter` (imports `PurityWilson`): the scalar content of Proposition C.1 of the same paper, with the Rayleigh bound `λ₀ ≥ a₀^M/J_s` and the min–max comparison `λ_f ≤ E ∏_l a_{f(l)}` (`E = e^{max V_s}`) as hypotheses; (C.1) with the trace on the left and no eigenvalue-wise comparison is `PurityTemporalOperator.temporal_trace_tensor`; eigenvalues of `C_b` are indexed by `f : Fin M → ι` over a finite label set listed with multiplicity. `ratio_le`, `trace_le`: `Σ (λ_i/λ₀)^T ≤ (E J)^T Σ (c_i/A)^T`. `tensor_sum`: `Σ_f (∏_l a_{f(l)}/a₀^M)^T = (Σ_ρ (a_ρ/a₀)^T)^M`. `temporal_comparison`: (C.1). `vacuum_ge_one`: `∫ V dν = 0` gives `∫ e^{−V} dν ≥ 1` (Jensen). `char_sum_ge_one`: the trivial label makes the character sum `≥ 1`. `log_lower`: `log((e^{bP} J)^T S^M) ≥ bPT` for `J, S ≥ 1`. Not formalized: the concrete operators `C_b` and `Π` on `L²(G^M)` and `C_b Π = Π C_b` from gauge covariance; for a general compact group, the fusion rule with `N_σ ≥ 0`, `∫ Re χ_σ ≥ 0`, `|Re χ_ρ| ≤ d_ρ` and `∫ k_b Im χ_ρ = 0` (for SU(2), the fusion rule with `N_σ ≥ 0` and `|χ_n| ≤ n + 1` are `PuritySU2Character.fusion` and `PuritySU2Character.abs_chi_le`; `Re ∫ Tr σ ≥ 0` for every continuous representation is `PurityRepCharacter.re_integral_trace_nonneg`; `|Re Tr U| ≤ d` for the defining representation is `GaugeGroup.abs_re_trace_repM_le`; from the fusion rule and `∫ Re χ_σ ≥ 0`, `a_ρ ≥ 0` is `PurityCharCoeff.charCoeff_exp_nonneg`, and for the characters `Re Tr σ` of continuous representations of every compact gauge group, with no fusion rule, it is `PurityRepCharacter.charCoeff_nonneg`; `|a_ρ| ≤ a₀` from `|χ_ρ| ≤ d_ρ` is `PurityCharCoeff.abs_charCoeff_le`; `a₀ > 0` is `PurityCharCoeff.a0_pos`; `J_s ≥ 1` at SU(N) is `PurityVacuumMean.vacuum_ge_one_SU`); `J_s > 0` for a general compact group; convergence of the representation sum and the limit from finite truncations; the test-vector inputs `C_b 1 = a₀^M 1`, `Π 1 = 1` and `‖D⁻¹1‖² = J_s` for the concrete operators; the order-`bT⁴` estimate. The SU(2) Bessel form `a_j/a₀ = I_{2j+1}(b)/I₁(b)` is `PuritySU2Bessel.coeff_div_coeff_zero`. `∫ V_s = 0`, `J_s ≥ 1` and `max V_s = b|P_s|` on one slice are `PurityVacuumMean`. In finite dimension, for `C ≥ 0`, a projection `P` commuting with `C`, symmetric `D` with `‖D y‖² ≤ c ‖y‖²` and `K = D C P D`, `Tr (K/l)^T ≤ (c J)^T Tr (C/A)^T` for `l ≥ A/J`, and the Rayleigh bound `λ₀(K) ≥ A/J` from a test vector `w` with `D w = u`, `P u = u`, `C u = A u`, `‖w‖² ≤ J ‖u‖²`, the sorted eigenvalue bound `λ_j(K) ≤ c λ_j(C)`, and, for `C` diagonal in a label basis, `Tr (K/l)^T ≤ (c J)^T (Σ_ι (a/a₀)^T)^M` (`temporal_trace_tensor`), are `PurityTemporalOperator`.
- `PurityTemporalOperator` (imports `PurityWeyl`, `PuritySpectralTrace`, `PurityCharacter`): the operator steps of Proposition C.1 of the same paper on a finite-dimensional inner product space, for `C ≥ 0`, an orthogonal projection `P` with `C P = P C`, `B = C P`, symmetric `D` with `‖D y‖² ≤ c ‖y‖²` and `K = D B D`. `pow_succ_mul`, `trace_pow_mul_comm`: `(X Y)^{T+1} = X (Y X)^T Y` and `Tr (X Y)^T = Tr (Y X)^T`. `sqrtOp`, `sqrtOp_apply_eigenvectorBasis`, `sqrtOp_isSymmetric`, `sqrtOp_mul_self`, `exists_sqrt`: for `B ≥ 0`, `S x = Σ_i √λ_i ⟪e_i, x⟫ e_i` is symmetric with `S S = B`. `inner_mul_proj`, `isPositive_mul_proj`, `form_proj_le`: `⟪C P x, x⟫ = ⟪C P x, P x⟫`, so `0 ≤ C P ≤ C` as forms. `re_trace_pow_le`: `0 ≤ X ≤ Y` as forms gives `re Tr X^T ≤ re Tr Y^T` (through `PurityWeyl.eigenvalues_le_add`). `re_trace_conj_pow_le`: `re Tr K^T ≤ c^T re Tr C^T`, through `Tr K^T = Tr (S D² S)^T` and `S D² S ≤ c B ≤ c C` as forms. `temporal_trace_ratio_le`: for `A, J > 0` and `l ≥ A/J`, `re Tr (K/l)^T ≤ (c J)^T re Tr (C/A)^T`, with `l = λ₀(K)`, `A = a₀^M`, `J = J_s` this is (C.1) with right side `Tr (C/A)^T`. `temporal_trace_tensor`: when `C` has an orthonormal eigenbasis indexed by labels `f : Fin M → ι` with eigenvalues `∏_l a(f l)`, `re Tr (K/l)^T ≤ (c J)^T (Σ_{i∈ι} (a_i/a₀)^T)^M` for `l ≥ a₀^M/J`; with `ι` indexing a basis of one link factor (each representation `ρ` listed `d_ρ²` times) the right side is `(c J)^T (Σ_ρ d_ρ² (a_ρ/a₀)^T)^M` (`PuritySpectralTrace.re_trace_pow_eq_of_basis`, `PurityCharacter.tensor_sum`). `rayleigh_top`: for `A, J > 0`, `u ≠ 0` with `P u = u`, `C u = A u` and `w` with `D w = u`, `‖w‖² ≤ J ‖u‖²`, `λ₀(K) ≥ A/J` (`PurityWeyl.le_tail` at index 0; the paper's test vector `D⁻¹1`). `temporal_trace_ratio_top`: under the hypotheses of both, `re Tr (K/λ₀)^T ≤ (c J)^T re Tr (C/A)^T` with `λ₀` the largest eigenvalue of `K`. `eigenvalues_conj_le`: `λ_j(D B D) ≤ c μ_j(B)` for `B ≥ 0`, eigenvalues sorted in decreasing order (through `PurityWeyl.exists_ne_zero_inter_map`); `eigenvalues_temporal_le`: `λ_j(K) ≤ c λ_j(C)` for `c ≥ 0`, the eigenvalue-wise comparison with `C P ≤ C` (`PurityWeyl.eigenvalues_le_add`).
- `PurityTrace` (imports Mathlib only): the scalar content of Proposition 6.1 of the same paper, for a kernel `A ∈ [a, b]`, `0 < a < b`, `B = b/a`, `τ = (B − 1)/(B + 1)`. `ratio_le`, `ratio_attained`, `attained_mem`: `(b − m)(m − a)/m² ≤ (b − a)²/(4ab)` on `[a, b]`, equality at `m = 2ab/(a + b) ∈ [a, b]`; `tau_identity`: `(b − a)²/(4ab) = τ²/(1 − τ²)`; `variance_ratio_le`, `variance_ratio_le_tau`: the second and third relations of (6.1), `Var(A)/Ā² ≤ τ²/(1 − τ²)` for any probability measure, from Mathlib's Bhatia–Davis `variance_le_sub_mul_sub`. `pow_le_sq_mul`: `x^T ≤ ε^(T−2) x²` at `0 ≤ x ≤ ε`, `T ≥ 2`. `tail_le`: (6.2) over a finite family, `r_j ∈ [0, τ]` gives `Σ r_j^T ≤ τ^{T−2} Σ r_j²`; `tail_le_of_sq`: with the hypothesis `Σ r_j² ≤ τ²/(1 − τ²)`, `Σ r_j^T ≤ τ^T/(1 − τ²)`; `thermal_trace_bound`: (6.3), with that hypothesis, `Tr R^T = 1 + Σ r_j^T` and `Tr R^{2T} ≥ 1`, `ψ_T ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^T/(1 − τ²))`. The Rayleigh bound, the Hilbert–Schmidt identity and (6.1) for `L²` operators are `PurityRayleigh`. Not formalized here: the spectral expansion of `Tr R^T` (in finite dimension it is `PuritySpectralTrace`), and `r_j ∈ [0, τ]` from Theorem 5.2.
- `PurityResponse` (imports Mathlib only): Proposition 6.4 of the same paper, for probability measures `ν₁, ν₂` and measurable actions `0 ≤ S₁ ≤ 2N`, `0 ≤ S₂ ≤ 4N`; in the Wilson instance `ν₁, ν₂` are the product Haar measures on the `T`- and `2T`-tori and `N = 6VT`. `mom ν g S b = ∫ g e^{−bS} dν`, `Z_b = mom ν 1 S b`, `gibbs ν S b = ⟨S⟩_b`, `eT N ν S b = ⟨S⟩_b/N`, `psi = 2 log Z_b(T) − log Z_b(2T)`. `hasDerivAt_mom`: `d/db ∫ g e^{−bS} = −∫ g S e^{−bS}` for bounded measurable `g, S` on a finite measure; `Z_pos`, `Z_zero`, `hasDerivAt_log_Z`: `Z_b > 0`, `Z_0 = 1`, `(log Z_b)' = −⟨S⟩_b`; `gibbs_mem`, `eT_mem`: `0 ≤ e_T(b) ≤ 2`; `continuous_gibbs`. `hasDerivAt_psi`, `continuous_response`: (6.6), `dψ/db = 2N(e_{2T} − e_T)`, continuous, so `ψ` is `C¹`; `psi_eq_integral`: (6.7), `ψ(b) = 2N ∫₀^b (e_{2T} − e_T)`; `psi_lipschitz`: (6.8), `|ψ(b) − ψ(b₀)| ≤ 4N|b − b₀|`. `certified`, `radius_eq`, `response_certified`: (6.9), a margin `ψ(b₀)/V ≤ ε_T/2` at `ε_T = 1/(161(48T)³)` gives `ψ(b)/V ≤ ε_T` for `|b − b₀| ≤ 1/(7728 · 48³ T⁴)`. `PurityResponseWilson` supplies the Wilson instance. `PurityResponseSmooth` gives `C^∞`.
- `PurityResponseWilson` (imports Mathlib, `RectWilsonG`, `GaugeWilson`, `PurityResponse`, `OpenAIDictionary`): Proposition 6.4 for the Wilson partition function of a compact gauge group `G` on the periodic box `L`. `SW = (sysRG G L).action/2` is the paper's action: `sysRG` sums over ordered direction pairs; the diagonal pairs contribute `φ(1) = 0` (`hol_μμG`), and `hol (j,i) = (hol (i,j))⁻¹` (`hol_swap`) with `wilsonDensityG_inv` makes each unoriented plaquette count twice (`action_eq_two_mul`). `action_le`, `SW_nonneg`, `SW_le`, `SW_le_VT`: `0 ≤ SW ≤ 12VT = 2N` with `N = 6VT`, `VT = ∏ L i` (`VT_eq`, `prod_eq`: `V = ∏_{j≠μ} L j`, `T = L μ`); `prod_double`: doubling `L μ` doubles `∏ L i`, so `SW G L′ ≤ 4N` with `N = 6VT` of the `T` box (`SW_le_VT'`). `mom_eq`: `Z_b = ZRG G L (b/2)` under the product Haar measure `nu G L`; `psi_eq`: `ψ(b) = 2 log ZRG G L (b/2) − log ZRG G L′ (b/2)`. `wilson_hasDerivAt`, `wilson_psi_eq_integral`, `wilson_lipschitz`, `wilson_response_certified`: (6.6)–(6.9) for these partition functions, with `|ψ(b) − ψ(b₀)| ≤ 24VT|b − b₀|` and the certified radius `1/(7728 · 48³ T⁴)`.
- `PurityResponseSmooth` (imports Mathlib, `PurityResponse`, `PurityResponseWilson`): the smoothness in Proposition 6.4. `hasDerivAt_mom` gives `d/db mom ν g S = −mom ν (g S) S` with `|g S| ≤ K M` again bounded and measurable, so `contDiff_mom` (induction on `n` through `contDiff_succ_iff_deriv`) makes `b ↦ ∫ g e^{−bS} dν` `Cⁿ` for every `n`, and `contDiff_mom_infty`, `contDiff_Z` give `C^∞`. With `Z_b > 0` (`Z_pos`): `contDiff_log_Z`, `contDiff_gibbs`, `contDiff_eT` and `contDiff_psi`, `ψ(b) = 2 log Z_b(T) − log Z_b(2T)` is `C^∞` for probability measures `ν₁, ν₂` and any bounded measurable `S₁, S₂`. For the Wilson model, `abs_SW_le` (`|SW| ≤ 2 (6 ∏ L i) = 12 ∏ L i`) gives `wilson_contDiff_ZRG`, `β ↦ ZRG G L β` is `C^∞` (through `mom_eq` at `b = 2β`), and `wilson_contDiff_psi`, `b ↦ 2 log ZRG G L (b/2) − log ZRG G L' (b/2)` is `C^∞`, for all `L, L'` and every compact gauge group `G`.
- `PurityCancel` (imports Mathlib only): the analytic content of Theorem 8.1 and Corollary 8.2 of the same paper. `Z_t = complexMGF A_t μ_t` for probability measures `μ_t` and measurable actions with `|A_T| ≤ 6VT`, `|A_{2T}| ≤ 12VT`; `psi = 2 log Z_T − log Z_{2T}` (principal logarithm), `radius V T = log(3/2)/(12VT)`. `cauchy_remainder`: `f` holomorphic on `|z| < R`, continuous on `|z| ≤ R`, `|f| ≤ M` on `|z| = R`, `f^{(n)}(0) = 0` for `n < T` give `|f(z)| ≤ M (|z|/R)^T/(1 − |z|/R)`. `differentiable_mgf`, `mgf_sub_one_le`, `mgf_near_one`: `|A| ≤ a` makes `Z` entire with `|Z(w) − 1| ≤ e^{|w|a} − 1 ≤ 1/2` for `|w|a ≤ log(3/2)`. `norm_log_le_log_two`: `|u| ≤ 1/2` gives `|log(1 + u)| ≤ log 2`; `psi_le`: `|2 log Z₁ − log Z₂| ≤ 3 log 2`; `near_one_on_disk`, `mgf_ne_zero`: `Z_T`, `Z_{2T}` are within `1/2` of `1` and nonzero on `|z| ≤ R`. `remainder`: (8.2), `|Ψ_T(z)| ≤ 3 log 2 (|z|/R)^T/(1 − |z|/R)` for `|z| < R`, with (8.1) as the hypothesis `Ψ_T^{(n)}(0) = 0` for `n < T`. `window_ratio`, `window_bound`, `corollary_window`: Corollary 8.2, `T ≥ 3`, `(2T)³ ≤ V < (48T)³` and `0 ≤ βW ≤ log(3/2)/(12000 · 48³ T⁴)` give `βW/R ≤ 1/1000` and `|Ψ_T(βW)| ≤ V/(161 (48T)³)`. `psi_nonneg_trace`: `λ_i ≥ 0`, `Σ λ_i^{2T} > 0` give `0 ≤ 2 log Σ λ_i^T − log Σ λ_i^{2T}`. (8.1) for the Wilson action with product Haar measure is `PurityWilsonChain.hvan_wilson`, by moment matching (`PurityMomentMatch`, `PurityTwoLift`) in place of the cumulant expansion (8.3); it holds at every transverse extent, so the side condition `s_j ≥ 2T` is not needed for it. Not formalized here: the principal logarithm as the analytic logarithm normalized at `0`. At odd `T = 2j + 1` the real counterpart `log Z(L′) ≤ 2 log Z(L)` holds for `RectWilsonG.ZRG` over a nontrivial gauge group at `β > 0` with every spatial extent at least `2` (`PurityOddTimeLower.ZRG_defect_odd_nonneg`, by positive semidefiniteness of the transfer operator, without the spectral sum), and `Ψ_T(2β) ≥ 0` at odd `T` on the `SU(N)` Wilson lattice, `N ≥ 2`, is `PurityOddTimeWilson.psi_nonneg_wilson_odd`. `PurityPlaqCount` supplies the product Haar measure, the action and its bounds, `Ψ_T(2β) = 2 log Z_T − log Z_{2T}` and `Ψ_T(2β) ≥ 0` at even `T`.
- `PurityMomentMatch` (imports `PurityCancel`): (8.1) of the same paper from moment matching. `order_psi`: for `f, g` analytic at `0` with `f 0 = g 0 = 1`, the analytic order of `2 log f − log g` at `0` is at least that of `g − f²`, from `(2 log f − log g)' = (2 f' (g − f²) − (g − f²)' f)/(f g)` near `0`. `vanish_psi`: the same for derivatives of order `< T`. `hvan_of_mgf`: `∀ n < T, (Z_{2T} − Z_T²)^{(n)}(0) = 0` gives the hypothesis `hvan` of `PurityCancel.remainder` and `PurityCancel.corollary_window`. `iteratedDeriv_mgf_zero`: `Z^{(n)}(0) = E[A^n]`. `iteratedDeriv_mgf_sub_sq`: `(Z_{2T} − Z_T²)^{(n)}(0) = E_{2T}[A^n] − Σ_{i ≤ n} C(n,i) E_T[A^i] E_T[A^{n−i}]`. `hvan_of_moments`: that moment matching for `n < T` gives `hvan`. The moment matching and `hvan` for a nearest-neighbour chain are `PurityTwoLift.moment_cover` and `PurityTwoLift.hvan_cover`. `PurityWilsonChain` identifies the Wilson action at circumference `T` with such a chain.
- `PurityTwoLift` (imports `PurityMomentMatch`): the moment matching behind (8.1) of the same paper, for a nearest-neighbour chain. Sites `x : Fin (m+1)` carry two i.i.d. variables `Z x : E × E`; for a bounded measurable bond weight `b`, `chain b Y = Σ_x b(Y_x, Y_{x+1})` is the cycle of circumference `T = m+1`, `cover b` the connected double cover (circumference `2T`, sheets exchanged on the bond `last → 0`), `trivialCover b` the trivial double cover, `trivialCover_eq`: `chain b Z^false + chain b Z^true`. `bond_cut`: the sheet relabelling `cut j (x,e) = (x, e ⊕ [x ≤ j])` with the site map `swapLe j` carries every cover bond off the fibre over `j` to a trivial-cover bond. `exists_missing`: a tuple of `n < T` bonds misses the bond based at some site. `lift_lift`: relabelling a tuple at its missed site is an involution. `measurePreserving_swapLe`. `moment_cover_eq_trivial`: `E[cover^n] = E[trivialCover^n]` for `n < T`. `moment_trivial`: `E[trivialCover^n] = Σ_{i ≤ n} C(n,i) E[chain^i] E[chain^{n−i}]` by independence. `moment_cover`: `E[cover^n] = Σ_{i ≤ n} C(n,i) E[chain^i] E[chain^{n−i}]` for `n < T`. `measurable_cover`, `abs_cover_le` (`|cover b| ≤ 2TC`). `hvan_cover`: `∀ n < T, Ψ^{(n)}(0) = 0` for `A_T = chain b`, `A_{2T} = cover b`, the hypothesis `hvan` of `PurityCancel.corollary_window`, through `PurityMomentMatch.hvan_of_moments`.
- `PurityPlaqCount` (imports `RectWilson`, `DoublingWilson`, `PurityCancel`): the plaquette action `A_t = (1/N) Σ_p Re Tr U_p` of §8 at `G = SU(N)` on the rectangular 4-torus with extents `L`, `p` a pair of directions `i < j` and a site, `U_p` the holonomy of `RectWilson.sysR`. `card_plaqs`: `6 ∏_i L_i` plaquettes. `abs_plaqAction_le`: `|A| ≤ 6 ∏_i L_i`, from `|Re Tr g| ≤ N`. `action_eq`: `S(U) = 12 ∏_i L_i − 2 A(U)` for `N ≠ 0` (`U_{ii} = 1`, `U_{ji} = U_{ij}⁻¹`); the paper's `βW` is `2β` for `RectWilson.ZR L N β`. `ZR_eq`: `Z(β) = e^{−12β ∏_i L_i} E[e^{2βA}]`. `psi_eq_log_ZR`: `Ψ_T(2β) = 2 log Z_{L₁}(β) − log Z_{L₂}(β)`, the four-volume term cancelling. `log_ZR_double_nonneg`, `psi_nonneg_wilson`: `Ψ_T(2β) ≥ 0` for `T = L₁ μ` even and `β ≥ 0`, the lower bound of Corollary 8.2, from `RectTransfer.ZR_eq_traceSeq` and the trace facts `DoublingWilson.facts`. `abs_plaqAction_le_slab`: `|A_t| ≤ 6Vt`, `t = L_μ`, `V = ∏_{i ≠ μ} L_i`. `measurable_plaqAction`. `corollary_window_wilson`: the bound `|Ψ_T(x)| ≤ V/(161(48T)³)` of `PurityCancel.corollary_window` with `μ_t` the product Haar measure on the links of `L₁` and of `L₂` (`L₁` with `L_μ` doubled) and `A_t` the plaquette action; the bounds and measurability are discharged, (8.1) is the hypothesis `hvan`. `PurityWilsonChain.corollary_window_wilson` is the same bound with `hvan` discharged.
- `PurityWilsonChain` (imports `PurityTwoLift`, `PurityPlaqCount`): (8.1) of the same paper for `G = SU(N)`, product Haar measure and the plaquette action. The 4-torus is a cycle of time slices in direction `μ`; a slice variable holds the axis links leaving the slice and the spatial links in it. `bondW μ T V V' = 6 ∏_j T_j − (spT(V) + 2 tpT(V, V'))/2` (`sliceAct`); `plaqAction_Φ`: `A(Φ W) = Σ_t bondW(W_t, W_{t+1})`, from `RectWilson.action_Φ` and `PurityPlaqCount.action_eq`. `plaqAction_chain`: at `L_μ = T` the action is `PurityTwoLift.chain bondW`. `kEquiv`: the slices of the cycle of circumference `2T` as `(x, e) : Fin T × Bool`, `t = x + T[e]`; `kEquiv_walk`: `t ↦ t+1` is the cover step `(x, e) ↦ (x+1, e ⊕ [x = last])`; `plaqAction_cover`: at `L_μ = 2T` the action is `PurityTwoLift.cover bondW`. `measurePreserving_reindex`, `measurePreserving_sheets`: the slice maps carry product Haar to product Haar; `complexMGF_comp`, `complexMGF_chain`, `complexMGF_cover`: the moment generating functions agree. `abs_bondW_le` (from `RectTransfer.sp_le`, `RectTransfer.tp_le`), `measurable_bondW`: `bondW` is bounded and measurable. `hvan_wilson`: `∀ n < T, Ψ_T^{(n)}(0) = 0`, from `PurityTwoLift.hvan_cover`. `corollary_window_wilson`: Corollary 8.2's bound `|Ψ_T(x)| ≤ V/(161(48T)³)` for the Wilson action with (8.1) discharged.
- `PurityTaylor` (imports `PurityBound`): Theorem 2.2, (2.3) and (2.4) from the excited-weight bound `x^T ≤ d(p)` (`x = λ₁/λ₀`), and Lemma 2.4 of the same paper. `gap_ratio_le`: `x ≤ d(p)^{1/T}`. `gap_log`: `1/2 < p < 1`, `x > 0`, `T > 0`, `x^T ≤ d(p)` give `(1/T) log((1 + √(2p − 1))/(1 − √(2p − 1))) ≤ −log x`, with `x = λ₁/λ₀`; `gap_log_sharp`: equality at `x = d(p)^{1/T}`. `F ε = −log((√(2e^{−ε} − 1) + 1)/2)`, `B ε = e^{F ε} − 1` as in (2.5); `B_eq`: `B = (1 − s)/(1 + s)` with `s = √(2e^{−ε} − 1)`; `B_eq_d`: `B ε = d(e^{−ε})`; `F_eq_log`: `F = log(1 + B)`; `s_zero`, `F_zero`, `B_zero`. `exp_neg_bound`: `|e^{−ε} − (1 − ε + ε²/2)| ≤ (2/9)|ε|³` for `|ε| ≤ 1`; `s_bound`: `|s ε − (1 − ε)| ≤ |ε|³` for `|ε| ≤ 1/4`. `B_bound`, `F_bound`: for `|ε| ≤ 1/4`, `|B ε − ε/2 − ε²/4| ≤ 2|ε|³` and `|F ε − ε/2 − ε²/8| ≤ 4|ε|³`; `B_taylor`, `F_taylor`: the same as `O(ε³)` at `0`; `hasDerivAt_F`, `hasDerivAt_B`: `F′(0) = B′(0) = 1/2`, via the generic `hasDerivAt_of_taylor`. `twoLevel_F` (`ε ≤ log 2`) and `F_twoLevel` (on `[0, log 2]`): `F` and `PurityBound.twoLevel` are inverse; `exp_neg_F`, `F_mem`, `twoLevel_mem`. Lemma 2.4: `F_strictMonoOn`, `F_convexOn` on `[0, log 2]`, and (2.6) `F_chord`: `F ε ≤ (F ε*/ε*) ε` for `0 < ε* ≤ log 2`, `0 ≤ ε ≤ ε*`.
