- `PurityRayleigh` (imports `JentzschKT`, `KernelPower`, `PurityTrace`): the operator steps of Proposition 6.1 of the same paper, (6.1), for a probability measure `μ`, a bounded measurable `D`, a jointly measurable `A` with values in `[a, b]`, `0 < a`, the kernel `k x y = D(x) A(x, y) D(y)` as the `L²(μ)` operator `K = kernelCLM`, and `S = ∫ D² > 0`; `dint w = ∫∫ D(x)² w(x, y) D(y)²`. `rayleigh`: `∫ (Kg) g ≤ ‖K‖ ∫ g²` for bounded measurable `g`; `rayleigh_dad`: `dint A ≤ ‖K‖ S` (`λ₀ ≥ Sµ`, `µ = dint A/S²`, trial function `D`). `hs_dad`, `traceSeq_two`: `‖K‖²_HS = KernelPower.hs μ k = ∫∫k² = dint (A²)`; for symmetric `A` this is `Tr K² = S²∫A²`. `dint_lin`, `dint_nonneg`, `dint_one` (`dint 1 = S²`), `weighted`; `dint_bounds`: `aS² ≤ dint A ≤ bS²`; `bhatia_davis`: `dint (A²) ≤ (a + b) dint A − abS²`. `trace_ratio_var`: the first relation of (6.1), `‖K‖²_HS/‖K‖² − 1 ≤ S² dint (A²)/(dint A)² − 1 = Var_{σ⊗σ}(A)/µ²`; `trace_ratio_le`: the first and second relations combined, `‖K‖²_HS/‖K‖² − 1 ≤ (b − a)²/(4ab)`, which for symmetric `A` and `R = K/‖K‖` is `Tr R² − 1 ≤ (b − a)²/(4ab)`. Helpers: `abs_A_le`, `abs_A_sq_le`. Not formalized here: the operator trace and `Tr K² = ∫∫k²` for a symmetric kernel, `‖K‖ = λ₀` as the top eigenvalue, the spectral expansion `Tr R^T = 1 + Σ r_j^T` (in finite dimension it is `PuritySpectralTrace`), and `r_j ∈ [0, τ]` from Theorem 5.2. (6.2) and (6.3) at even `T` for `L²` kernels are `PurityThermal`. On a compact space with continuous `D` and `A`, `‖K‖` is an eigenvalue (`JentzschKT.exists_pos_eigen`) and `|r_j| ≤ τ` (`PurityThermalCompact.norm_rem_le`).
- `PurityThermal` (imports `KernelPower`, `DefectBound`, `PurityTrace`, `PurityRayleigh`): (6.2) and (6.3) of the same paper at even times `T = 2j`, `j ≥ 1`, for a symmetric bounded measurable kernel `k` on a probability measure `μ` with a bounded measurable eigenfunction `φ`, `∫φ² = 1`, `Kφ = cφ`, `|c| = ‖K‖ > 0`, and the deflated kernel `rem k c φ = k − c φ⊗φ` with operator `R`, `‖R‖ ≤ τ|c|`. `hs_rem`, `hs_rem_ratio`: `‖rem‖²_HS = ‖K‖²_HS − c²`, so `‖rem‖²_HS/c² = ‖K‖²_HS/‖K‖² − 1`. `excited_le`: the relative excited weight at `T = 2j` is at most `τ^{T−2} ‖rem‖²_HS/c²`. `trace_excess_le`: with `‖K‖²_HS/‖K‖² − 1 ≤ U`, `‖K^j‖²_HS/‖K‖^T − 1 ≤ τ^{T−2} U`; `defect_le`: `ψ_T = 2 log ‖K^j‖²_HS − log ‖K^{2j}‖²_HS ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^{T−2} U)`. For the DAD kernel of `PurityRayleigh` with `0 < a < b`, `τ = (b/a − 1)/(b/a + 1)`: `pow_tail`: `τ^{T−2} · τ²/(1 − τ²) = τ^T/(1 − τ²)`; `trace_excess_dad`: (6.2), `‖K^j‖²_HS/‖K‖^T − 1 ≤ τ^T/(1 − τ²)`; `defect_dad`: (6.3), `ψ_T ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^T/(1 − τ²))`. The ratio `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖` (Theorem 5.2, (5.2), as an operator-norm bound on the deflated operator) and the bounded eigenfunction `φ` are hypotheses; positivity `K ≥ 0` is not used. Not formalized here: the identification of `‖K^j‖²_HS` with the operator trace `Tr K^{2j}` for symmetric `k` (odd `T`: `PurityOddTime`, `PurityOddTimeLower`, `PurityOddTimeWilson`). On a compact space with continuous `D` and `A`, the eigenfunction `φ` with `|c| = ‖K‖` and `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖` are derived in `PurityThermalCompact`.
- `PurityThermalCompact` (imports `KernelPower`, `KernelEigen`, `JentzschKT`, `PurityContraction`, `PurityThermal`): Theorem 5.2 of the same paper as an operator-norm bound, and (6.2), (6.3) at even `T` with no spectral hypotheses. `orth`, `eigen_of_rem`, `ratio_ne` (any probability space, `k` symmetric and bounded, `φ` a bounded eigenfunction with `∫φ² = 1`): an eigenfunction `g` of `rem k c φ` with eigenvalue `σ ≠ 0` satisfies `∫φg = 0`, is an eigenfunction of `k` with eigenvalue `σ`, and, when `∫g² = 1` and `φ` has no zeros, `g/φ` is not constant. On a compact second countable Borel space `X` with a probability measure, `k x y = D(x) A(x, y) D(y)` with `D` continuous, `0 < dl ≤ D ≤ du`, `A` continuous and symmetric with values in `[a, b]`, `0 < a < b`: `kcont`, `ksymm`, `klow` (`k ≥ dl² a`); `tau_eq`: `(b/a − 1)/(b/a + 1) = (b − a)/(b + a)`; `continuous_eigen`: a bounded measurable eigenfunction of `k` with nonzero eigenvalue is continuous. `norm_rem_le`: `‖K‖ > 0` is an eigenvalue of `K` with a continuous eigenfunction `φ ≥ m > 0`, `|φ| ≤ C/‖K‖`, `∫φ² = 1` (`JentzschKT.exists_pos_eigen`), and for every bound `Cr` on `rem k ‖K‖ φ` the deflated operator `R` satisfies `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖`, `τ = (b/a − 1)/(b/a + 1)`; `‖R‖` is attained at an eigenfunction of `R` (`KernelEigen.exists_eigen`), which is an eigenfunction of `k` with `g/φ` bounded and not constant, and (5.2) (`PurityContraction.eigen_le`) bounds its eigenvalue. `trace_excess_compact`: (6.2), `‖K^j‖²_HS/‖K‖^T − 1 ≤ τ^T/(1 − τ²)`; `defect_compact`: (6.3), `ψ_T ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^T/(1 − τ²))`, at `T = 2j`, `j ≥ 1`. Not formalized here: the identification of `‖K^j‖²_HS` with the operator trace `Tr K^{2j}` (odd `T`: `PurityOddTime`, `PurityOddTimeLower`, `PurityOddTimeWilson`); simplicity of `λ₀ = ‖K‖` as a stated theorem (it follows from `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖ < ‖K‖`); the energy-gap statement `−log τ` for `K ≥ 0`; the case `a = b`; the Wilson transfer kernel as a concrete instance (the composition of the bounds of `PurityWilson` with Theorem 5.2 is `PurityWilsonCompact`).
- `PurityWilsonCompact` (imports `KernelPower`, `PurityWilson`, `PurityThermalCompact`): the bounds of Lemma 5.3 and Theorem 5.4 of the same paper composed into Theorem 5.2 and (6.2), (6.3) on a compact space. `G` is a compact Borel space with a probability measure `ν`; the kernel is `A x y = ∫ exp (F x y g) dν(g)` for a jointly continuous `F` on `(X × X) × G`. `integrable_cont`: continuous functions on `G` are `ν`-integrable; `F_cont`, `expF_cont`: `F x y` and `exp ∘ F x y` are continuous. `A_cont`: `A` is continuous (dominated convergence, `X` compact second countable). `A_symm`: `F y x g = F x y (σ g)` for a measure-preserving `σ : G ≃ᵐ G` gives `A y x = A x y`. `A_lower`, `A_upper`: (5.5), `cl ≤ ∫ F x y dν` gives `exp cl ≤ A` (`PurityWilson.kernel_lower`), `F ≤ cu` gives `A ≤ exp cu`. With `k x y = D(x) A(x, y) D(y)`, `D` continuous, `0 < dl ≤ D ≤ du`, `cl < cu`, and `τ = (exp cu − exp cl)/(exp cu + exp cl)`: `norm_rem_le` (Theorem 5.2, `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖`), `trace_excess` ((6.2)), `defect` ((6.3)) at `T = 2j`, `j ≥ 1`. At `cu = 3βL³`, `cl = 3βL³q/d` with `β, L, d > 0`, `q < d` (`wilson_lt`: `cl < cu`): `wilson_norm_rem_le`, `wilson_trace_excess`, `wilson_defect` with `τ = tanh(3βL³(1 − q/d)/2)`. `tail_mono`: `s^T/(1 − s²)` is monotone in `s ∈ [0, 1)`; `wilson_defect_uniform`: for `0 ≤ q`, `ψ_T ≤ 2 log(1 + t^T/(1 − t²))` with the uniform ratio `t = tanh(3βL³/2)`. The hypotheses `cl ≤ ∫ F` and `F ≤ cu` stand for the Haar average `∫ Re Tr(U_e g_y V_e g_x⁻¹) dg = q` and `Re Tr W ≤ d`. The concrete Wilson transfer kernel over a gauge group, with the pointwise bounds `cl = −2β tpMax`, `cu = 0`, is `PurityRectWilson`. The Haar average `∫ Re Tr = q` for the concrete kernel is `PurityHaarProjection.integral_tpG`, and its composition with this module, ratio `tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`, is `PurityRectWilsonFixed`. Not formalized here: `‖K^j‖²_HS = Tr K^{2j}` (odd `T`: `PurityOddTime`, `PurityOddTimeLower`, `PurityOddTimeWilson`); simplicity of `λ₀` as a stated theorem; the case `q = d` (`a = b`), excluded by `q < d`; the step to `Δ_lat ≥ −log tanh(3βL³/2)`, which needs `K ≥ 0` and `λ₁ > 0` (for `kTG`, `PurityKernelPositive.kTG_quad_nonneg` and `PurityLam1Pos.lam1_pos`; at the ratio `tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`, with simplicity of `λ₀`: `PurityLatticeGap`); the remaining items of the `PurityWilson` list (gauge invariance of the top eigenfunction, the rank-one kernel at `β = 0`).
- `PurityRectWilson` (imports `RectTransferG`, `PurityWilsonCompact`): Theorem 5.2 and (6.2), (6.3) of the same paper for the gauge-projected transfer kernel `RectTransferG.kTG` of the rectangular torus over a gauge group `G`, `k = D·A·D` with `D V = exp(−β sp(V)/2)` and `A V V′ = ∫ exp(−2β tp(A, V, V′)) dA` over Haar measure on the axis links, `β > 0`, positive spatial extents. `hF`, `hFs`, `hcl`, `hcu`, `hlt`, `hDc`, `hDl`, `hDu` are the hypotheses of `PurityWilsonCompact` for this kernel: the exponent is jointly continuous, inverting the axis links (`RectTransferG.tpG_inv`, Haar-preserving) swaps `V` and `V′`, the inversion preserving Haar measure (`RectTransferG.measurePreserving_invAxG`), and `−2β tpMax ≤ −2β tp ≤ 0` pointwise (`hcl` integrates the lower bound; `tpMax_pos`: `tpMax > 0`). `ratio_eq`: `(1 − e^{−2β tpMax})/(1 + e^{−2β tpMax}) = tanh(β tpMax)`. `norm_rem_le_of`, `trace_excess_of`, `defect_of`: the composition with `PurityWilsonCompact` at `β ≥ 0` and any lower bound `cl < 0` of `∫ −2β tp dA`, `τ = (1 − e^{cl})/(1 + e^{cl})`; `ZRG_defect_of`: a bound on the doubling defect of `kTG` at `T = 2j` bounds `2 log Z(L) − log Z(L′)`. `norm_rem_le`, `trace_excess`, `defect`: `‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖`, (6.2), (6.3) at `T = 2j`, `j ≥ 1`, with `τ = tanh(β tpMax)`, below the ratio `tanh(β(spMax + 2tpMax))` of the SU(N) time-doubling bound `ApertureGap.aperture_gap`. `ZRG_defect`: for time extents `L μ = 2j`, `L′ μ = 4j` and the same spatial extents, `2 log Z(L) − log Z(L′) ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^{2j}/(1 − τ²))` for the Wilson partition function `RectWilsonG.ZRG` (`RectTransferG.ZRG_eq_traceSeq`). The Haar average of the temporal plaquette term is `PurityHaarProjection.integral_tpG`. Not formalized here: the items of the `PurityWilsonCompact` list (odd time extents: `PurityOddTimeWilson`).
- `PurityHaarProjection` (imports `RectTransferG`, `PurityWilson`): Lemma 5.3 of the same paper, the Haar average of the temporal plaquette term. For a compact gauge group `G` with representation `ρ = repM G` of dimension `d`, `haarProj G = P = ∫ ρ(g) dg` entrywise. `repM_mul_haarProj`, `haarProj_mul_repM`, `haarProj_mul_self`: `ρ(h)P = Pρ(h) = P² = P` (left and right invariance of `probHaar`); `integral_repM_inv`: `∫ ρ(g⁻¹) dg = P`. `fixedSub G = {v | ∀ g, ρ(g)v = v}`, `fixedRank G = q = dim (fixedSub G)`; `haarProj_mulVec_of_mem`, `range_haarProj`: `P` maps onto `fixedSub G` and fixes it; `trace_haarProj`: `Tr P = q` (`LinearMap.IsProj.trace`); `fixedRank_le`: `q ≤ d`; `fixedRank_lt`: `q < d` for nontrivial `G` (`ρ` injective). `integral_pi_two`: two distinct coordinates of a product Haar measure are independent. `measurePreserving_shiftAt`: left translation of one coordinate preserves product Haar measure. `integral_trace_mul`: `∫ Tr(ρ(g) Y) dg = Tr(P Y)`. `integral_trace_link`, `integral_wilson_link`: if `M(A)` does not depend on `A_y`, `∫ Tr ρ(A_y M(A)) dA = Tr P = q` and `∫ (1 − Re Tr ρ(A_y M(A))/d) dA = 1 − q/d`. `integral_trace_edge`, `integral_wilson_edge`: the case `M(A) = a A_x⁻¹ b`, `x ≠ y`. `trace_expand`, `integral_sum4`: `Tr(Y C Z D)` as a fourfold entry sum, and the integral of a fourfold finite sum. `integral_tpG`: with every transverse period at least `2` (`RectWilson.tsh_ne_self`), `∫ tp(A, V, V′) dA = (tpMax/2)(1 − q/d)` over `hAG`; `integral_exponent`: `∫ −2β tp dA = −β tpMax (1 − q/d)`; `gkG_ge_fixed`, `kTG_lower_fixed` take the same hypothesis. `gkG_ge_fixed`: Jensen (`PurityWilson.kernel_lower`) gives `exp(−β tpMax (1 − q/d)) ≤ gkG` at every real `β`; `kTG_lower_fixed`: `kTminFixed = e^{−β spMax} e^{−β tpMax (1 − q/d)} ≤ kTG` at `β ≥ 0`. Its composition with `PurityWilsonCompact` is `PurityRectWilsonFixed`.
- `PurityVacuumMean` (imports `PurityHaarProjection`, `PurityCharacter`, `GaugeInstances`): the spatial vacuum of Appendix C of the same paper on one slice, `V_s = (b/2)(n − sp)` with `n = nPl μ T = (c² − c)|TT|` (`c` transverse directions), so `|P_s| = n/2` (`sp` lists each plaquette in both orientations, equal by `GaugeGroup.wilsonDensityG_inv`, and equal directions give `0`), and `V_s = (b/d) Σ_{p ∈ P_s} Re Tr U_p`. `nPl_eq`: `n = 6|TT|`. `integral_spTG`: with every transverse period at least `2`, `∫ sp dV = n(1 − q/d)` over `hSG` for every gauge group (each plaquette with distinct directions has the link `V(ν, y)` once; equal directions give `0`; `PurityHaarProjection.integral_wilson_link`). `fixedRank_SU`: `q = 0` for `SU(N)`, `N ≥ 2`, in the defining representation (the centre scalar `e^{2πi/N}·1` fixes no nonzero vector). `integrable_spTG`; `integral_vacuum_SU`: `∫ V_s dV = 0`; `vacuum_ge_one_SU`: `J_s = ∫ e^{−V_s} dV ≥ 1` (`PurityCharacter.vacuum_ge_one`). `spTG_one`: `sp(1) = 0`; `vacuum_le_max`, `vacuum_one`: `V_s ≤ b|P_s|` at `b ≥ 0`, with equality at `V = 1`.
- `PurityCharCoeff` (imports `MKBound`, `PurityKernelPositive`): the character coefficients of Appendix C of the same paper, `a_j = MKBound.charCoeff μ χ d (e^{b g}) j = (1/d_j) ∫ e^{b g} χ_j` and `a₀ = ∫ e^{b g}`, on a finite measure space with real characters `χ_j`, `|g| ≤ 1`, `|χ_j| ≤ B_j`. `χ_j` stands for `Re χ_π`: `k_b(g⁻¹) = k_b(g)` and inversion invariance of Haar measure give `∫ k_b Im χ_π = 0`, so the paper's `a_π` is real and equals `charCoeff` at `Re χ_π`; the fusion rule for `χ_π` passes to real parts since `g` and `N_σ` are real. `integrable_pow_mul`: `gⁿ χ_j` is integrable. `moment_nonneg`: fusion positivity (`g χ_j = Σ_{σ∈s} N_σ χ_σ`, `N_σ ≥ 0`, for every `j`) and `∫ χ_σ ≥ 0` give `∫ gⁿ χ_j ≥ 0`, by induction on `n`. `integral_exp_mul_nonneg_of_moment`: `∫ e^{b g} χ ≥ 0` for `b ≥ 0` when `∫ gⁿ χ ≥ 0` at every `n` (`PurityKernelPositive.expTrunc` expands into `Σ_{n<N} bⁿ/n! ∫ gⁿ χ`; dominated convergence with bound `e^b B`). `integral_exp_mul_nonneg`: the same for `χ_j` under fusion positivity. `charCoeff_exp_nonneg`: `a_j ≥ 0` for `d_j > 0`. `abs_charCoeff_le`: `|a_j| ≤ ∫ k` for `k ≥ 0` integrable and `|χ_j| ≤ d_j`. `a0_pos`: `a₀ > 0` on a nonzero finite measure. The fusion rule and `∫ χ_σ ≥ 0` (from orthogonality, `∫ χ_σ = δ_{σ,0}`) for a concrete group enter as hypotheses; `PurityRepCharacter` proves the moment positivity without them.
- `PurityRepCharacter` (imports `GaugeGroup`, `PurityCharCoeff`): Appendix C of the same paper for every compact gauge group `G` (`GaugeGroup`, defining representation `ρ_F = repM G` of dimension `d`, `g = Re Tr ρ_F / d`) and every continuous matrix representation `σ : G →* Matrix ι ι ℂ`. `proj σ = ∫ σ`; `proj_mul`: `P_σ σ(h) = P_σ` (right invariance); `proj_mul_self`: `P_σ² = P_σ`; `integral_trace`: `∫ Tr σ = rank P_σ`; `re_integral_trace_nonneg`. `kron`, `conjRep`, `repHom`: the tensor product, the conjugate and `ρ_F` as monoid homs; `continuous_kron`, `continuous_conjRep`, `continuous_repHom`: continuity; `trace_kron` (`Tr (σ ⊗ τ) = Tr σ Tr τ`) and `trace_conjRep` (`Tr σ̄ = conj Tr σ`). `pow_succ_mul_trace`: `(2 Re Tr ρ_F)ⁿ⁺¹ Tr σ = (2 Re Tr ρ_F)ⁿ (Tr (ρ_F ⊗ σ) + Tr (ρ̄_F ⊗ σ))`. `re_integral_pow_mul_trace_nonneg`: `Re ∫ (2 Re Tr ρ_F)ⁿ Tr σ ≥ 0`, by induction on `n` over all representations. `moment_nonneg`: `∫ gⁿ Re Tr σ ≥ 0`. `abs_g_le`: `|g| ≤ 1`. `integral_exp_mul_nonneg`: `∫ e^{b g} Re Tr σ ≥ 0` for `b ≥ 0` (`PurityCharCoeff.integral_exp_mul_nonneg_of_moment`). `charCoeff_nonneg`: `(1/d_σ) ∫ e^{b g} Re Tr σ ≥ 0` for every `d_σ > 0` (in particular `d_σ = dim σ`). No fusion rule, orthogonality relation or classification of irreducibles enters; `SU(N)` at every `N` is an instance.
- `PurityConvEigen` (imports `PurityRepCharacter`): the convolution eigenvalue `a_π` of Appendix C of the same paper and its `d_π²` eigenfunctions, for every compact gauge group `G` and every continuous matrix representation `σ : G →* Matrix ι ι ℂ`, `d = card ι`. `mint F`: the entrywise Haar integral; `mint_mul_right`, `mint_mul_left`, `trace_mint`, `mint_left`, `mint_right`, `mint_conj`. `Irred σ`: every subspace of `ι → ℂ` invariant under all `σ g` is `⊥` or `⊤`. `schur`, `eq_trace_smul`: for irreducible `σ`, a matrix commuting with every `σ g` equals `(Tr A / d) • 1` (an eigenspace of `A` is invariant). `mint_comm`: `∫ F` commutes with `σ` when `F (x h x⁻¹) = σ x F(h) σ x⁻¹`. `conjAvg_eq`: `∫ σ(g) E σ(g⁻¹) dg = (Tr E / d) • 1`. `orthogonality`: `∫ σ(g)ᵢⱼ σ(g⁻¹)ₗₖ dg = δᵢₖ δⱼₗ / d`; `orthogonality_unitary`: `∫ σ(g)ᵢⱼ conj σ(g)ₖₗ dg = δᵢₖ δⱼₗ / d` when `σ(g⁻¹) = σ(g)ᴴ`. `linearIndependent_coeff`, `finrank_span_coeff`: the `d²` coefficients `g ↦ σ(g)ᵢⱼ` are linearly independent and span a space of dimension `d²`. `mint_kernel`: `∫ k(h) σ(h⁻¹) dh = a_σ · 1` for irreducible `σ` and a continuous class function `k`. `conv_coeff`: for a continuous class function `k`, `(k ⋆ f)(g) = ∫ k(h) f(h⁻¹ g) dh` maps each coefficient `σᵢⱼ` to `a_σ σᵢⱼ`, `a_σ = (1/d) ∫ k(h) Tr σ(h⁻¹) dh` (`eigenval`); `conv_eigen_multiplicity`: every function in the span is an `a_σ`-eigenfunction, so the `a_σ`-eigenspace has dimension at least `d²`. `PurityL2.eq_zero_of_inner_coeff` gives completeness of the irreducible coefficients in `L²(G)`; `PurityKernelSeries.hasSum_dim_sq_eigenval` gives `Σ_π d_π² a_π = k(1)` for the Wilson kernel.
- `PurityPeterWeyl` (imports `PurityRepCharacter`): the uniform density of unitary representation coefficients in `C(G)`, for every compact gauge group `G`, by Stone–Weierstrass (the `C(G)` step toward the Peter–Weyl density used in Appendix C of the same paper). `IsCoeff f`: `f g = Tr (A σ(g))` for a continuous unitary representation `σ` on a finite type. `isCoeff_const`, `isCoeff_add` (direct sum `dsum`), `isCoeff_mul` (`PurityRepCharacter.kron`), `isCoeff_star` (`PurityRepCharacter.conjRep`), `isCoeff_entry` (the entries of `repM G`). `coeffAlg`: the star subalgebra of `C(G, ℂ)` generated by the entries of `repM G`; `isCoeff_of_mem`: each element is a coefficient; `coeffAlg_separatesPoints` (`repM_injective`); `coeffAlg_dense`: `coeffAlg` is dense; `coeff_dense`: every continuous `f : G → ℂ` is uniformly within any `ε > 0` of a coefficient of a continuous unitary representation. The density of the coefficients of irreducible representations is `PurityReducible.irredSpan_dense`. The passage to `L²(G)` is `PurityL2.eq_zero_of_inner_coeff`.
- `PurityReducible` (imports `PurityConvEigen`, `PurityPeterWeyl`): complete reducibility at the level of coefficients, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`: every coefficient `g ↦ Tr (A σ(g))` of a continuous unitary representation `σ` is a finite sum of coefficients of continuous irreducible unitary representations. `inv_eq_star`, `inv_eq_conjTranspose`: `σ(g⁻¹) = σ(g)ᴴ` for unitary `σ`. `blockRep p τ`: the `p`-block of a representation whose matrices vanish between `p` and `¬p`; `blockRep_mem` (unitary), `continuous_blockRep`, `trace_block` (`Tr (B τ(g))` is the sum of the two block traces). `conjHom σ U`: `g ↦ U⋆ σ(g) U` for unitary `U`. `mint_mulVec_mem`: for continuous `F`, `(∫ F) x` lies in every subspace containing all `F(g) x`. `conjAvg_isHermitian`: `∫ σ(g) E σ(g)ᴴ dg` is Hermitian for Hermitian `E`. `exists_block`: a reducible unitary `σ` is conjugate by a unitary `U` to a representation whose entries vanish between the level sets of a real function `d` taking at least two values; in the proof `d` is the eigenvalue list of `∫ σ(g) v v⋆ σ(g)ᴴ dg` for a nonzero `v` in an invariant subspace `W ≠ ⊤`, a matrix that commutes with `σ` (`PurityConvEigen.conjAvg_comm`), has range in `W` and trace `‖v‖²` (spectral theorem, `Matrix.IsHermitian.spectral_theorem`). `irredSpan G`: the span of the coefficients of continuous irreducible unitary representations; `coeff_mem_irredSpan` (induction on the dimension), `isCoeff_mem_irredSpan`; `irredSpan_dense`: every continuous `f : G → ℂ` is uniformly within any `ε > 0` of an element of `irredSpan G` (`PurityPeterWeyl.coeff_dense`). The passage to `L²(G)` is `PurityL2.eq_zero_of_inner_coeff`.
- `PurityL2` (imports `PurityReducible`): completeness of irreducible coefficients in `L²(G, probHaar G)`, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`. `continuous_of_mem_irredSpan`: every element of `irredSpan G` is continuous. `irredCM G`: the continuous maps whose underlying function lies in `irredSpan G`; `irredCM_dense`: dense in `C(G, ℂ)` for the uniform norm. `toL2`: `C(G, ℂ) → L²(G)`; `coeFn_toL2`: `toL2 G c = c` almost everywhere. `toL2_irredCM_dense`: the image of `irredCM G` is dense in `L²(G)` (`ContinuousMap.toLp_denseRange`; `probHaar G` is regular). `eq_zero_of_inner_irredCM`: an element of `L²(G)` orthogonal to the image of `irredCM G` is zero; `eq_zero_of_inner_coeff`: an element of `L²(G)` orthogonal to every coefficient `g ↦ Tr (B τ(g))` of every continuous irreducible unitary representation `τ` is zero.
- `PuritySchurCross` (imports `PurityReducible`): orthogonality between irreducible representations, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`. `rint`: the entrywise Haar integral of a rectangular-matrix-valued function. `Equivalent σ τ`: some nonzero `T` satisfies `T σ(g) = τ(g) T` for every `g`; for irreducible `σ`, `τ` this is equivalence. `avg_intertwines`: `∫ τ(g) E σ(g⁻¹) dg` intertwines `σ` and `τ`. `bijective_of_intertwines`: a nonzero intertwiner between irreducible representations is a linear bijection; `card_eq_of_equivalent`, `trace_eq_of_equivalent`: equivalent irreducible representations have equal dimension and equal character. `integral_cross`: for inequivalent `σ`, `τ`, `∫ τ(g)ₐᵦ σ(g⁻¹)꜀ₑ dg = 0`; `integral_cross_unitary`: for unitary `σ`, `∫ τ(g)ₐᵦ conj σ(g)ₑ꜀ dg = 0`. `integral_trace_mul_conj`: `∫ χ_τ conj χ_σ dg` as a double sum of entry integrals. `integral_char_self`: `∫ |χ_σ|² dg = 1` for continuous irreducible unitary `σ` of positive dimension. `integral_char`: for continuous irreducible `σ`, `τ` with `σ` unitary of positive dimension, `∫ χ_τ conj χ_σ dg` is `1` if `σ` and `τ` are equivalent and `0` otherwise.
- `PurityCharDecomp` (imports `PuritySchurCross`): character decomposition into irreducibles, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`. `IrrRep G`: a continuous irreducible unitary representation of positive dimension. `char_decomp`: for continuous unitary `σ` there is a finite family `R` of `IrrRep G` with `Σ_j dim R_j = dim σ` and `Tr σ(g) = Σ_j Tr R_j(g)` for every `g`. `mult σ r = ∫ χ_σ conj χ_r dg`; `mult_eq`: it is `Σ_j [r ≃ R_j]` for any finite family `R` of `IrrRep G` with `χ_σ = Σ_j χ_{R_j}`. `finsum_dim_mult`: for a family `F` such that every `IrrRep G` is equivalent to exactly one `F i`, `Σᶠ_i dim (F i) · mult σ (F i) = dim σ`.
- `PurityKernelSeries` (imports `PurityCharDecomp`, `CharacterExpansion`): the eigenvalues of the Wilson kernel `k_b(g) = exp(b Re Tr U(g) / dim G)`, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`; `dim G` is the dimension `d` of the defining representation `U`. `exists_tensorPow`: `(2 Re Tr U(g))^n` is the character of a continuous unitary representation of dimension `(2 dim G)^n`, namely `(U ⊕ Ū)^{⊗n}`. `mom n r = ∫ (2 Re Tr U)^n conj χ_r dg`; `exists_mom_eq`: it is a natural number. `hasSum_dim_mom`: for a family `F` such that every `IrrRep G` is equivalent to exactly one `F i`, `Σ_i dim (F i) · mom n (F i) = (2 dim G)^n`. `hasSum_dim_sq_eigenval`: for such `F` and every real `b`, `Σ_i dim (F i)² · eigenval k_b (F i) = e^b`, the value `k_b(1)`, by the power series of `k_b` in `2 Re Tr U`, dominated convergence, and the absolutely convergent double series summed in both orders.
- `PurityEigenspace` (imports `PurityL2`, `PurityCharDecomp`): the eigenspaces of convolution by a continuous class function `k` on `L²(G, probHaar G)`, for Appendix C of the same paper and every compact gauge group `G`. `integral_kernel_conj`: `∫ k(h) conj Tr(B τ(hg)) dh = a_τ conj Tr(B τ(g))` for continuous irreducible unitary `τ`. `conv_trace`: `k ⋆ Tr(C τ(·)) = a_τ Tr(C τ(·))`. `integral_conj_conv`: `∫ conj c · (k ⋆ f) = e ∫ conj c · f` for integrable `f` and continuous `c` with `∫ k(h) conj c(hg) dh = e conj c(g)` (Fubini and left invariance). `integral_conj_eq_zero`, `inner_coeff_eq_zero`: an integrable (resp. `L²`) solution of `k ⋆ f = a f` is orthogonal to every coefficient of a continuous irreducible unitary `τ` of positive dimension with `a_τ ≠ a`. `conv_congr_ae`: `k ⋆ f` depends only on the almost-everywhere class of `f`. `coeffSpan G k a`: the closed span in `L²(G)` of the coefficients `g ↦ Tr(B r.τ(g))` of the `r : IrrRep G` with `a_{r.τ} = a`; `conv_toL2_coeff`, `toL2_coeff_mem_coeffSpan`: each coefficient of `r : IrrRep G` solves `k ⋆ f = a_{r.τ} f` almost everywhere and lies in `coeffSpan G k a_{r.τ}`; `inner_coeffSpan_eq_zero`: a coefficient of `τ` with `a_τ ≠ a` is orthogonal to `coeffSpan G k a`. `mem_coeffSpan`: every `f ∈ L²(G)` with `k ⋆ f = a f` almost everywhere lies in `coeffSpan G k a`. `eq_zero_of_eigen`: if no `r : IrrRep G` has `a_{r.τ} = a`, then `f = 0`.
- `PuritySU2Character` (imports `PurityCharCoeff`, `SU2DiskLaw`): the coefficients of Appendix C of the same paper at `G = SU(2)` in the Weyl variable `x = cos θ`, with `g = x`, `χ_n = U_n(x)`, `d_n = n + 1` and Haar measure on class functions `(2/π)(1 − x²) dmeasureT` (`Polynomial.Chebyshev.measureT`, density `(1 − x²)^{-1/2}` on `[-1, 1]`). `clamp` (`SU2DiskLaw.clamp1`, the identity on `[-1, 1]`) makes `|g| ≤ 1` hold everywhere; `integral_comp_clamp`: it leaves `measureT` integrals unchanged. `chi n x = (2/π)(1 − x²) U_n(x)`. `one_sub_sq_mul_eval_U`: `(1 − x²) U_n = (T_n − T_{n+2})/2`. `integral_chi`: `∫ chi n ∂measureT = δ_{n,0}`. `fusion`: `x U_0 = ½ U_1`, `x U_{m+1} = ½ U_{m+2} + ½ U_m`. `abs_eval_U_le`: `|U_n(y)| ≤ n + 1` for `|y| ≤ 1`. `abs_chi_le`: `|chi n x| ≤ n + 1` for all real `x` (`chi` is defined through `clamp`). `coeff b n = (1/(n+1)) ∫ e^{b x} chi n ∂measureT`; `coeff_nonneg`: `a_n ≥ 0` for `b ≥ 0` (`PurityCharCoeff.charCoeff_exp_nonneg`); `abs_coeff_le`: `|a_n| ≤ a₀` (`PurityCharCoeff.abs_charCoeff_le`); `coeff_zero_pos`: `a₀ > 0`. `integral_suTau` (the Weyl integration formula): for continuous `f` and `τ g = ½ Re Tr g`, `∫ f(τ g) dg = (2/π) ∫_{-1}^{1} f(t) √(1 − t²) dt` over `SU(2)` Haar measure (`SU2DiskLaw.conv_eq_ellInt` at `V = 1`, `g = diag(i, −i)`); `integral_haar`: `∫ f(τ g) dg = ∫ weight · f ∂measureT`; `integral_haar_U`: `∫ U_n(τ g) dg = δ_{n,0}`; `coeff_eq_haar`: `a_n = (1/(n+1)) ∫ e^{b τ g} U_n(τ g) dg`. `weightSum n z = Σ_{k=0}^{n} z^{n−k} z^{−k}` (the weights `n, n − 2, …, −n`); `eval_U_weightSum`: `U_n((z + z⁻¹)/2) = weightSum n z` for `z ≠ 0`; `det_sub_eq`: `det (z·1 − g) = z² − Tr g · z + 1`; `suTau_eq_trace`: `τ g = ½ Tr g` in `ℂ`; `exists_eigenvalue`: `g` has an eigenvalue; `eval_U_suTau_eq_weightSum`: `U_n(τ g) = weightSum n z` at every eigenvalue `z` of `g`. `PuritySU2SymPower.symChar_eq_eval_U` identifies `weightSum n z` with the trace of `g` on `Sym^n ℂ²`, so `U_n(τ g)` is the spin-`n/2` character and `coeff` is the paper's `a_π` at `G = SU(2)`.
- `PuritySU2SymPower` (imports `PuritySU2Character`): the space `Sym^n ℂ²` with the substitution action, and the trace of `g ∈ SU(2)` on it. `act A = aeval (X_i ↦ Σ_j A i j X_j)`, the substitution action on `MvPolynomial (Fin 2) ℂ`; `act_mul`: `act (A B) = act B ∘ act A` (a right action; `g ↦ act g⁻¹` is the left representation); `act_one`. `sym n = span {X₀^{n−k} X₁^k : k ≤ n}`; `sym_eq`: `sym n` is the degree-`n` homogeneous submodule. `symBasis n`: the monomial basis. `symLin n A`: the action restricted to `sym n`, with `symLin_mul`, `symLin_one`; `symChar n A = trace (symLin n A)`. `symChar_eq`: `symChar n A = Σ_k coeff_{X₀^{n−k} X₁^k} (act A (X₀^{n−k} X₁^k))`; `symChar_conj`: `symChar n (P⁻¹ A P) = symChar n A` for invertible `P`; `symChar_of_lower_eq_zero`: `symChar n T = Σ_{k=0}^{n} T₀₀^{n−k} T₁₁^k` for `T 1 0 = 0`. `symChar_eq_weightSum`: `symChar n g = weightSum n z` at every eigenvalue `z` of `g ∈ SU(2)` (conjugation by `P = [v | (−v̄₁, v̄₀)]`, `det P = |v|² ≠ 0`, with `v` an eigenvector, makes `g` triangular with diagonal `z, z⁻¹`). `symChar_eq_eval_U`: `symChar n g = U_n(τ g)`.
- `PuritySU2Bessel` (imports `PuritySU2Character`): the SU(2) Bessel form of Appendix C of the same paper. `besselI n b = (1/π) ∫₀^π e^{b cos θ} cos (nθ) dθ`, the modified Bessel function `I_n`, `n ∈ ℕ`, in its integral form (DLMF 10.32.3) (Mathlib has no modified Bessel functions). `coeff_mul`: `(n + 1) a_n = I_n(b) − I_{n+2}(b)`, from `x = cos θ`, `T_n(cos θ) = cos (nθ)` and `(1 − x²) U_n = (T_n − T_{n+2})/2`. `mul_besselI_sub`: `b (I_n(b) − I_{n+2}(b)) = 2(n + 1) I_{n+1}(b)`, by integration by parts. `coeff_eq_besselI`: `a_n = 2 I_{n+1}(b)/b` for `b ≠ 0`. `besselI_one_ne_zero`: `I₁(b) ≠ 0` for `b ≠ 0`. `coeff_div_coeff_zero`: `a_n/a₀ = I_{n+1}(b)/I₁(b)` for `b ≠ 0`, the paper's `a_j/a₀ = I_{2j+1}(b)/I₁(b)` at `n = 2j`.
- `PurityRectWilsonFixed` (imports `PurityHaarProjection`, `PurityRectWilson`): Theorem 5.2 and (6.2), (6.3) of the same paper at the ratio of Lemma 5.3 and Theorem 5.4, for `RectTransferG.kTG` over a nontrivial gauge group, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`. The lower bound is `cl = −β tpMax (1 − q/d)`, from the equality `PurityHaarProjection.integral_exponent`; `hlt`: `cl < 0` (`fixedRank_lt`, `PurityRectWilson.tpMax_pos`); `ratio_eq`: `(1 − e^{cl})/(1 + e^{cl}) = tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`. From `PurityRectWilson.norm_rem_le_of` and its companions: `norm_rem_le` (`‖R‖ ≤ τ‖K‖`), `trace_excess` ((6.2)), `defect` ((6.3)) at `T = 2j`, `j ≥ 1`, and `ZRG_defect` (`2 log Z(L) − log Z(L′) ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^{2j}/(1 − τ²))` for `L μ = 2j`, `L′ μ = 4j`), `τ = tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`. Odd time extents are `PurityOddTime`. The step to `Δ_lat` is `PurityLatticeGap`.
- `PurityKernelPositive` (imports `KernelInjective`, `RectTransferG`): `K ≥ 0` for the step from Theorem 5.2 of the same paper to `Δ_lat`. `kTG_quad_nonneg`: for any gauge group, `β ≥ 0` and every `u` integrable over `hSG`, `∫∫ u(x) kTG(x, y) u(y) ≥ 0`. `SliceKernelDefinite.sliceKernel_form_nonneg` covers `SU(N)` and continuous gauge-invariant `u`, and `WilsonSlab.form_nonneg_wilson` covers `SU(N)` on all of `L²`; this module covers any `GaugeGroup` and every integrable `u`, by averaging over a second axis configuration. Abstract part: for coordinates `|φ| ≤ 1` on `X × Y` and a probability measure `ν`, `avgKern ν φ c x y = ∫∫ exp(c ∑ₚ φₚ(x, b) φₚ(y, a)) dν(a) dν(b)`; `quad_nonneg`: its form is nonnegative on integrable `W` at `c ≥ 0`. The truncated exponential `expTrunc N` expands through `KernelInjective.coordKernel_pow` (`expTrunc_coordKernel`, `avgKernTrunc_eq`) into `∑_{n<N} cⁿ/n! ∑_s ampl_s(x) ampl_s(y)`, so `quadTrunc_nonneg`; dominated convergence with bounds built from `exp(c|P|)` gives `tendsto_avgKernTrunc`, `tendsto_quadTrunc`. Wilson part: `wilsonDensityG_mul_inv` (`wilsonDensityG(g h⁻¹) = 1 − (1/d) ∑_q c_q(h) c_q(g)`, entry coordinates `entryCoord`); `tpTG_eq`, `crossEnergy_act` (gauge invariance of the crossing energy), `gkG_eq_avg` (left invariance of `hAG`), `gkG_eq_avgKern` (`gkG = e^{−2β|SpT|} avgKern hAG actCoord (2β/d)`). The passage to `Δ_lat` is `PurityLatticeGap`. `λ₁ > 0` is `PurityLam1Pos`.
- `SliceKernelDefiniteG` (imports `PurityKernelPositive`, `SliceKernelDefinite`): `SliceKernelDefinite` for every compact gauge group `G`, the definiteness input to `λ₁ > 0` in Theorem 5.2 of the same paper. `sp_gaugeAct`, `measurePreserving_gaugeAct`: the spatial action is invariant under the axis gauge action `PurityKernelPositive.gaugeAct`, which preserves the product Haar measure; `entryCoord_sep`, `planeCoordG_sep`: the entry coordinates of `repM G` separate the points of `G` (`repM_injective`) and of a slice; `crossEnergy_planeCoord`, `gkG_eq_axis`: `gkG = e^{−2β|SpT|} ∫ e^{(2β/d) coordKernel planeCoordG V (A · V')} dA`; `sliceKernelG_form_eq`: for continuous gauge-invariant `u`, `∫∫ u(V) u(V') kTG(V, V') = e^{−2β|SpT|} · crossForm (Haar) planeCoordG (2β/d) (w · u)`, `w = e^{−β spTG/2}`; `sliceKernelG_form_eq_zero_iff`: at `β > 0` the form vanishes on a continuous gauge-invariant `u` exactly at `u = 0` (`KernelInjective.weighted_crossForm_eq_zero_iff`). `λ₁ > 0` is `PurityLam1Pos`.
- `PurityLam1Pos` (imports `PurityExcitedTop`, `SliceKernelDefiniteG`, `PurityVacuumMean`): `λ₁ > 0` in Theorems 5.2 and 5.4 of the same paper, for the Wilson slice transfer kernel `kTG` and every nontrivial compact gauge group. `kernel_eq_of_norm_rem_eq_zero`, `form_eq_zero_of_norm_rem_eq_zero` (probability space): at `‖R‖ = 0`, `R` the operator of `rem k c φ = k − c φ ⊗ φ`, `∫ k(z, y) g(y) dy = c φ(z) ∫ φ g` almost everywhere for bounded measurable `g`, and the form of `k` vanishes on `g ⊥ φ`; `form_prod_eq`: the product-measure form is the iterated form; `norm_rem_pos` (`β > 0`, spatial extents at least `2`, any bounded measurable `φ`, any `c`): `‖R‖ > 0`, since otherwise the form vanishes on the gauge-invariant `u = (∫ φ sp) − (∫ φ) sp ⊥ φ` (or `u = 1` at `∫ φ = 0`), so `u = 0` (`sliceKernelG_form_eq_zero_iff`) and `sp ≡ sp(1) = 0`, against `∫ sp = n (1 − q/d) > 0`; `lam1_pos`: `PurityExcitedTop.lam1` with `0 < λ₁ ≤ τ λ₀` and `λ₁` the eigenvalue of an excited eigenfunction.
- `PurityLatticeGap` (imports `KernelEigen`, `PurityKernelPositive`, `PurityRectWilsonFixed`): the lattice energy gap of Theorem 5.2 (last sentence) and (5.6) in Theorem 5.4 of the same paper for `RectTransferG.kTG` over a nontrivial gauge group, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`. `excited`: `λ₀ = ‖K‖ > 0` has a continuous eigenfunction `φ ≥ m > 0` with `∫ φ² = 1`, and every eigenfunction `g` of `kTG` that is bounded, measurable, `∫ g² = 1`, `∫ φ g = 0`, with eigenvalue `σ ≠ 0` (so `g ∈ (ker K)^⊥`), has `0 < σ ≤ τ λ₀ < λ₀` (so `λ₀` is simple) and `−log τ ≤ −log(σ/λ₀)`, `τ = tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`; `gap_pos`: `−log τ > 0` (`PurityWilson.neg_log_tanh_pos`). `σ > 0` is `eigen_nonneg_of_form` with `PurityKernelPositive.kTG_quad_nonneg`; `σ ≤ τ λ₀` is `rem_eigen` (`g` is an eigenfunction of the deflated kernel `rem kTG λ₀ φ`), `abs_eigen_le_norm` (`|σ| ≤ ‖R‖` on any probability space) and `PurityRectWilsonFixed.norm_rem_le`. `tau_le` (second inequality of (5.6)): `τ ≤ tanh(β tpMax/2)` for `β ≥ 0`; `gap_uniform`: `0 < −log tanh(β tpMax/2) ≤ −log τ`. The largest excited eigenvalue `λ₁` is `PurityExcitedTop`. `λ₁ > 0` is `PurityLam1Pos`.
- `PurityExcitedTop` (imports `PurityLatticeGap`, `PurityThermalCompact`): the largest excited eigenvalue `λ₁` of Theorem 5.4 of the same paper, the norm of the deflated operator `R` of `rem k c φ = k − c φ ⊗ φ`. `top`: on a compact second countable Borel space with a probability measure, for a continuous symmetric bounded kernel with nonnegative form `∫ (∫ k x y g y) g x ≥ 0` for integrable `g`, and `φ` continuous, bounded, `∫ φ² = 1`, `Kφ = c φ`, every bounded measurable eigenfunction `g ⊥ φ`, `∫ g² = 1`, has `|σ| ≤ ‖R‖` (`PurityLatticeGap.abs_eigen_le_norm`, `PurityLatticeGap.rem_eigen`), and if `‖R‖ > 0` there is a bounded measurable `ψ ⊥ φ`, `∫ ψ² = 1`, with `Kψ = ‖R‖ ψ` (`KernelEigen.exists_eigen` on `rem`, `PurityThermalCompact.orth`, `PurityThermalCompact.eigen_of_rem`, sign from `PurityLatticeGap.eigen_nonneg_of_form`). `lam1`: for `RectTransferG.kTG` over a nontrivial gauge group, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`, `λ₀ = ‖K‖ > 0` with its eigenfunction `φ ≥ m > 0`, and there is `λ₁` with `0 ≤ λ₁ ≤ τ λ₀`, `τ = tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`, `|σ| ≤ λ₁` for every excited eigenvalue, and, when `λ₁ > 0`, `λ₁` is the eigenvalue of an excited eigenfunction. `KernelPower.rem_cont`, `KernelPower.rem_symm` give continuity and symmetry of `rem`. `λ₁ > 0` (that `kTG` is not the rank-one kernel `λ₀ φ ⊗ φ`) is `PurityLam1Pos`.
- `PurityTailIdentity` (imports `KernelPower`): (D.2) and (D.3) of Appendix D of the same paper. On a probability space, for a bounded measurable kernel `k` and an orthonormal family `φ₀, …, φ_{m−1}` of bounded measurable functions, `gram μ k φ i j = ⟨φᵢ, K φⱼ⟩` and `proj μ k φ x z = Σᵢⱼ Gᵢⱼ φᵢ(x) φⱼ(z)`, the kernel of `P_m K P_m` (`proj_eq`). `sq_lin`: `∫ (Σⱼ aⱼ φⱼ)² = Σⱼ aⱼ²`. `tail` (D.2), the matrix certificate: `hs (k − proj) = hs k − Σᵢⱼ Gᵢⱼ²`, with `hs` the iterated integral `∫∫ h²` of `KernelPower`; no symmetry of `k` is used. `bessel`: `Σᵢⱼ Gᵢⱼ² ≤ hs k`. `tail_le`: `hs k ≤ U` and `L ≤ Σᵢⱼ Gᵢⱼ²` give `hs (k − proj) ≤ U − L`. `res_sq`: `∫ (k(x,·) − Σⱼ aⱼ φⱼ)² = ∫ k(x,·)² − 2 Σⱼ aⱼ (K φⱼ)(x) + Σⱼ aⱼ²`, the pointwise step of `tail` and `tail_right`. `tail_right`: `hs (k − Σᵢ (K φᵢ) ⊗ φᵢ) = hs k − Σᵢ ‖K φᵢ‖²`, the kernel of `K − K P_m`. `tail_left`, the tail identity (from `tail_right` and `KernelPower.hs_swap`): for symmetric `k`, `hs (k − Σᵢ φᵢ ⊗ K φᵢ) = hs k − Σᵢ ‖K φᵢ‖²`, that is `‖(I − P_m)K‖²_HS = Tr K² − Tr(P_m K²)`. `window` (D.3): `0 ≤ d ≤ exp(V/(322 (48T)³)) − 1` gives `2 log(1 + d) ≤ V/(161 (48T)³)`. Not formalized here: the numerical certification of `U`, `L` and `d` for `kTG`.
- `PurityOddTime` (imports `CyclicKernel`, `SectorDefect`, `PurityThermal`, `PurityRectWilsonFixed`): (6.2) and (6.3) of Proposition 6.1 of the same paper at odd `T = 2j + 1`, `j ≥ 1`, where the trace is the cyclic integral `CyclicKernel.cyc`. `trace_iter_le`: `|∫ r^{∗(2j+1)}(x, x)| ≤ ‖R‖^{2j−1} ‖r‖²_HS` for a bounded measurable kernel `r`, by Cauchy–Schwarz on `r^{∗j} ∗ r^{∗(j+1)}` and `KernelPower.hs_iter_le`; `cyc_odd`: `cyc(2j+1) = c^{2j+1} + ∫ r^{∗(2j+1)}(x, x)` for `r = k − c φ ⊗ φ`; `cyc_succ_ge`, `cyc_succ_pos`: `cyc(n+1) ≥ m^{n+1} > 0` for a bounded measurable kernel bounded below by `m > 0`. `trace_excess_odd_le`, `defect_odd_le`: with `‖R‖ ≤ τ c`, `c = ‖K‖ > 0` and `‖K‖²_HS/‖K‖² − 1 ≤ U`, `cyc(T)/‖K‖^T − 1 ≤ τ^{T−2} U` and, for `cyc(T) > 0`, `ψ_T ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^{T−2} U)`. For `RectTransferG.kTG` over a nontrivial gauge group, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`, with `U = τ²/(1 − τ²)` from `PurityRectWilsonFixed.trace_excess` at `T = 2`: `trace_excess_odd` ((6.2)), `defect_odd` ((6.3)) and `ZRG_defect_odd` (`2 log Z(L) − log Z(L′) ≤ 2 log(1 + τ^{2j+1}/(1 − τ²))` for `L μ = 2j + 1`, `L′ μ = 2(2j + 1)`), `τ = tanh(β tpMax (1 − q/d)/2)`. With `PurityRectWilsonFixed` at `T = 2j`, (6.2) and (6.3) for `kTG` hold at every integer `T ≥ 2`.
- `PurityOddTimeLower` (imports `PurityOddTime`, `KernelGap`, `FluxSectorBound`, `PurityKernelPositive`): the lower bound `ψ_T ≥ 0` of Proposition 6.1 of the same paper at odd `T = 2j + 1`, `j ≥ 1`. For a symmetric positive semidefinite operator `A` on a real inner product space, `a_n = ⟪A^n v, v⟫ ≥ 0` and `a_{n+l} ≤ ‖A‖^l a_n` (`inner_pow_nonneg`, `inner_pow_add_le`, from `‖A u‖² ≤ ‖A‖ ⟪A u, u⟫`); `form_sub_nonneg`: `⟪u, A u⟫ − c ⟪e, u⟫² ≥ 0` for a unit eigenvector `A e = c e`. For a bounded measurable symmetric kernel `k` with positive semidefinite operator `K`, bounded eigenfunction `φ`, `∫ φ² = 1`, eigenvalue `c`: `rem_apply`, `rem_form_nonneg`: `R u = K u − c ⟪u, φ⟫ φ` is positive semidefinite; `iter_diag_eq`: `r^{∗(i+3)}(x, x) = ⟪R^{i+1} r(·, x), r(·, x)⟫`; `trace_rem_nonneg_and_hs_le`: `t = ∫ r^{∗(2j+1)}(x, x) ≥ 0` and `‖r^{∗(2j+1)}‖²_HS ≤ ‖R‖^{2j+1} t`; `defect_odd_nonneg`: `ψ_{2j+1} ≥ 0` for `c > 0`, `‖R‖ ≤ c`. For `RectTransferG.kTG` over a nontrivial gauge group, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`: `kTG_form_nonneg` (the operator is positive semidefinite, from `PurityKernelPositive.kTG_quad_nonneg`), `defect_odd_nonneg_kTG` and `ZRG_defect_odd_nonneg` (`log Z(L′) ≤ 2 log Z(L)` for `L μ = 2j + 1`, `L′ μ = 2(2j + 1)`).
- `PurityOddTimeWilson` (imports `PurityOddTimeLower`, `PurityPlaqCount`, `GaugeInstances`, `HaarVariance`): the lower bound `Ψ_T(2β) ≥ 0` of Corollary 8.2 of the same paper at odd `T = 2j + 1`, `j ≥ 1`, on the `SU(N)` Wilson lattice, `N ≥ 2`, `β > 0`, every spatial extent at least `2`. `ZRG_SU_eq_ZR`: `RectWilsonG.ZRG` at `G = SU(N)` is `RectWilson.ZR` (definitional, as `GaugeInstances.wilsonDensityG_SU` is); `nontrivial_SU`: `SU(N)` at `N ≥ 2` is nontrivial (`HaarVariance.flipEl`); `log_ZR_double_nonneg_odd`: `2 log Z(L₁) − log Z(L₂) ≥ 0` for `L₁ μ = 2j + 1`, `L₂ μ = 2 L₁ μ`, from `PurityOddTimeLower.ZRG_defect_odd_nonneg`; `psi_nonneg_wilson_odd`: `Ψ_T(2β) ≥ 0`, through `PurityPlaqCount.psi_eq_log_ZR`.
- `PurityDegenerate` (imports `PurityWilson`): Section 6.3 of the same paper. `hasSum_neg_log_tanh`: `−log tanh x = 2 Σ_{j≥0} e^{−2(2j+1)x}/(2j+1)` for `x > 0` (from `hasSum_log_sub_log_of_abs_lt_one` at `y = e^{−2x}`); `bound_six_four`: (6.4), `2e^{−X} ≤ −log tanh(X/2) ≤ 2e^{−X}/(1 − e^{−2X})` for `X > 0`, `X = 3βL³`. `tendsto_scaled`: if `X(a) ≥ κ/a³` on `0 < a < 1` for some `κ > 0` (a fixed physical length `ℓ ~ La` with `β` bounded below), `a⁻¹ · (−log tanh(X(a)/2)) → 0` as `a → 0⁺`, through `scaled_le`: `a⁻¹ · (−log tanh(X/2)) ≤ 2a²/(κ(1 − e^{−2κ}))`; `tendsto_atTop`, `tendsto_volume`: `−log tanh(X/2) → 0` as `X → ∞`, so at fixed `β > 0` the bound tends to `0` as `L → ∞`. The two-point family `Ka a = [[a⁻², 1], [1, a⁻²]]` on two points with counting measure (a constant rescaling of the measure leaves `r_a` and the purity unchanged): `Ka_pos` (positive entries), `form_pos` (`⟨v, K_a v⟩ > 0` for `v ≠ 0`, `0 < a < 1`), `Ka_injective`, `mulVec_top`, `mulVec_excited` (eigenvalues `λ₀ = a⁻² + 1` on `(1, 1)`, `λ₁ = a⁻² − 1` on `(1, −1)`); `ratio_eq`: `λ₁/λ₀ = r_a = (1 − a²)/(1 + a²) = (a⁻² − 1)/(a⁻² + 1)`, the formula `(b − a)/(b + a)` of Lemma 5.1 at the largest and smallest entries `a⁻²`, `1` of `K_a`; `rate_bounds`: `2a/(1 + a²) ≤ −a⁻¹ log r_a ≤ 2a/(1 − a²)`, so `tendsto_rate` (`−a⁻¹ log r_a → 0`) and `tendsto_rate_div` (`−a⁻¹ log r_a ∼ 2a`); `trace_pow`: `Tr K_aⁿ = λ₀ⁿ + λ₁ⁿ`; `purity_eq`: `Tr K_a^{2T}/(Tr K_a^T)² = (1 + s²)/(1 + s)²`, `s = r_a^T`; `tendsto_pow_floor`, `tendsto_purity`: at `T_a = ⌊t/a⌋`, `t ≥ 0`, `r_a^{T_a} → 1` and the thermal purity tends to `1/2` as `a → 0⁺`.

## Superseded or restated

Do not cite the declarations in this section as results.

### Duplicates

- `GibbsSpec` and `InfiniteLattice` define the same objects. `Transfer.GNS` (a real quotient) and `GNSHilbert` (a complex completion) are related by `GNSCompare`.
- Every module of the group port restates an `SU(N)` module (`RectWilson` and `RectWilsonG`, `ClayWiring` and `ClayWiringG`, `ObserverOSLinked` and `ObserverOSG`); `ClayWiringG.clayStatementAt_of_inputs_viaG` recovers the `SU(N)` wiring exactly.
- `KnabeCriterion` (the Wilson application) and `KnabeEngine` (the engine), by design. `StripRow` is superseded by `StripWiden` for the width and kept for `StripWidth.strip_width_poly`.
- The Dobrushin–Shlosman block has near-parallel variants: `ObserverEnergyM`, `ObserverEnergyMClose`, `ObserverEnergyMDob`, `ObserverEnergyCellM`, `ObserverEnergyCellW1`, `ObserverEnergyCellCond`, `ObserverEnergyCellCondW`, `LocalPotential`, `LocalPotentialSharp`, `PotKnabe`, `PotSharpCluster`, `ObserverKnabe`, `ObserverErgodic`, `WeightedErgodic`, `OverlapErgodic`. `BlockDynamicStep` takes the block gap as a free function, so with a constant gap its capstone restates its conclusion; `BlockGapDef` defines the gap from the measure and is the one to use.

### Conditional forms replaced by unconditional ones

- `LayerSplit.TwoDCharacter N β` (the cited character expansion of the two-dimensional torus, not proved) is a hypothesis only of superseded forms: `AnisoTorus.decoupled_vanishes`, `DecoupledSplit.decoupled_vanishes_of_twoD`, `MixAnchor.weakMixAnchor_of_twoD`, `MixAnchor.strongEnd_of_aniso_twoD`, `MixPlane.segment_first_stall`, `MixPlane.axis_mem_vanishSet`. Use `TwoDVanish.twoD_vanishes`, `TwoDVanish.decoupled_vanishes`, `TwoDVanish.weakMixAnchor`, `TwoDVanish.strongEnd_of_aniso`, `StripClose.segment_first_stall`, `StripClose.axis_mem_vanishSet`, which do not read it. The named input `AnisoTorus.DecoupledFactor` is proved by `DecoupledSplit.decoupledFactor`.
- Routes to `StrongEnd` from unproved bounds: `LayerSplit.strongEnd_of_layerTransport`, `MixCut.strongEnd_of_mixCutBound`, `MixEqui.strongEnd_of_mixEquiUp`, `MixCutTest.strongEnd_of_varGap`.

### Refuted or vacuous inputs in the import list

Each is in the import list because later modules state its failure. None is a hypothesis of `OneInput.clayStatement_of_strongEnd` or `ExistClose.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_open'`, and none of the modules of those two statements names any of them.

- `ScaleControlRun` and `MultiScale.Endpoint`: `InputAudit.not_scaleControlRun` proves `¬ ScaleControlRun` and `InputAudit.not_multiScaleEndpoint` proves `¬ MultiScale.Endpoint`, each under the hypothesis `InputAudit.ZeroFluxLevelsUnbounded 2 D` (unboundedly many zero-flux levels near the ground in the window slices). That hypothesis is argued in the `EndpointAudit` module text, not proved; the refutation is therefore conditional, and the two inputs should be treated as unusable, not as open.
- `ScaleIdentityNoGo`: `not_substrateCoreAt_identity` and `not_substrateCoreAt_of_id` prove that the bundle `SU2Best.SubstrateCoreAt` is false at the identity observer and at any flow that is eventually the identity. It says nothing about the observer of the target.
- `ExistenceFlow.flatFlow`: the flow that replaces every link in a growing box by the identity. `ExistenceFlow.classMoments_flatFlow` proves that it meets the class-moment input, while its flowed fields are eventually constant, so the capstones that take class moments with a free flow are vacuous there (the vacuity is argued in the module; `ObserverCarries.not_clayStatementAt_flatFlow` proves that the clauses at the two-loop tower exclude it).
- `ObserverBlockReading`: `obsRegimeW_blockRead_vacuous` proves that `ObserverRegime.ObsRegimeW` holds for the pinned block reading, at every flow and every finite block set, with the trivial comparison constants `vacC`, `vacQ`; so it carries no content there.
- Also vacuous or failing, in the modules' own statements: `AsymptoticScaling.free_spacing_scaling_is_also_vacuous`; `BulkBound` (not met at `blFam`); `CompositesObserver.not_covers_and_repTieAll`; `FlowComp` (its remainder reduction is circular, per its docstring); `SunFamLeak` (`PlaqLeak` open at `sunFam` and `obsW`, with a conditional refutation of the multi-scale strong reading if the leak holds); the one-sided variants in `ZoomStep` and `BondMove` (`BondMove.upperToy_not_step`, `BondMove.lowerToy_not_step`).
- `CompositesBuild.clayStatementAt_SU_build` leaves the off-tensor composites unrenormalized, and `CompositeNondeg` records that the non-degeneracy clause then fails; `CompositesRen` and `CompositesRenAll` are the repair. Do not cite `clayStatementAt_SU_build` as an existence capstone.
- `BoxGapBE.clay15_strong_without_box` concludes "for some `βsc > 0`" with no value in the statement; the number in its docstring is not a theorem.

### Restatements of the target

The library has 151 distinct theorem names beginning with `clayStatement`, in 89 modules, and 90 beginning with `clay_`. They form about 60 capstones that differ only in the bundle of residual hypotheses, many of them mutually implied. Families:

- Tower capstones with different residuals, composed by `OneInput`: `LinkAlongG`, `TowerOpen`, `CouplingGen`, `FlowAlong`, `FieldSweep`, `ObserverTower`, `ObserverTowerPM`, `ObserverTowerCL`, `BlFamCore`; also `ConstantPhysicsLine.clayStatementOf_of_reads`, `ConsolidatedRoot.clayStatementOf_of_open`, `ConsolidatedRoot.clayStatementOf_of_flow` (the one without the bridge, from `FlowInputsN` and `AsymScalingG`), `ScaleLightCone.clayStatementOf_of_zoomSettles`, `ZoomBase.clayStatementOf_of_zoomBase`, `ApertureModes.clayStatementOf_of_apertureModes`, `ApertureCount.clayStatementOf_of_apertureCount`, `BlFamCore.clayStatementOf_of_blFamCore`, `StrongAnchor.clayStatementOf_of_noStall`.
- Ledger capstones at the two-loop tower (cluster 4b), `OneBridge.clayStatementAt_SU_oneBridge`, and `OneLoopInput.clayStatement_of_oneLoop_tower` (from a cited perturbative input `OneLoopZoom N` in place of `StrongEnd N`).
- Small restatements of the bridge: `TowerParamFree`, `StrongEndVanishing`, `SelfSimilarStep`, `LineSeedsVanishing`, `ReferenceUnit`; `MixPlane.strongEnd_iff_diag`.

The ones to use are `OneInput.clayStatement_of_strongEnd` (Theorem 1.1) and `ExistClose.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_open'` (with the concrete existence witness). `MeasuredSpacing.clayStatementMeas_of_dy` and `MeasuredSpacing.clayStatementMeasOf_of_of` lift any capstone at the two-loop tower to the measured-tower target under the assumed asymptotic scaling `MeasuredSpacing.ScalingSU` (or `MeasuredTower.AsymScalingG` at one group); `ClayMeasDirect.clayStatementOf_of_inputsAlongG` states the target from the inputs along a measured tower without it.

### Vacuous capstones

`ReflFloor.headline_hypotheses_inconsistent` proves that `StrongEnd N` and the floor residual `ReadPos.ReflFloorAt N βs (2 log 2)` (frequently near `0⁺`, along every coupling tower) give `False`; `ReflFloor.not_floor_residual` and `ReflFloor.not_upper_residual` refute the fixed-level residuals at every level. So these capstones have inconsistent hypotheses and prove nothing: `ReadPos.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_floor`, `BandUpper.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_upper`, `BandClose.clayStatement_of_strongEnd_reads`, and `OneLoopInput.clayStatement_of_oneLoop`, `OneLoopInput.clayStatement_of_oneLoop_open`, `OneLoopInput.clayStatement_of_oneLoopAbove` (each input gives `StrongEnd N`). They stay in the library, marked vacuous in their docstrings, because `ReflFloor` states its refutation against them; the other lemmas of `ReadPos`, `BandClose` and `BandUpper` are unaffected and are used by `ReflFloor` and `MassBand`. The band of `MeasuredTower.MeasTowerG` is not refuted: its level is existential per tower.

## Removed from the library

Modules removed from the root for the release. Each is kept, byte for byte, in this repository's git history (the parent of the checkpoint that removed it).

- `ClayMeasLift`: twenty lifts of the two-loop-tower capstones to the measured-tower target under the assumed asymptotic scaling of the tensor mass. Each was one application of `MeasuredSpacing.clayStatementMeas_of_dy` or `MeasuredSpacing.clayStatementMeasOf_of_atG` to a capstone that stays; no hypothesis bundle is lost, and no module imported it.

The other candidates of the prune pass stay: the vacuous capstones above (kept as the statements `ReflFloor` refutes), the conditionally refuted inputs and their audit (`ScaleControlRun`, `MultiScale`, `EndpointAudit`, `InputAudit`, `SunFamLeak`), the no-go and vacuity results (`ScaleIdentityNoGo`, `ExistenceFlow`, `ObserverBlockReading`, `BulkBound`, `FlowComp`, `CompositesBuild`), the superseded conditional forms and the Dobrushin–Shlosman variants (each imported by modules that stay), and the tower capstones that `OneInput` composes.

## Keeping names live

A renamed or deleted declaration leaves a document pointing at nothing. `research/code/certify/doc_cites_live_declarations.py` reads every backticked token of a document that looks like a Lean identifier (it contains `_` or `.` and is not a file name) and checks it against the Lean sources in two tiers: the last component of the name must occur in the sources or in `research/code`, and it must still occur once Lean comments are stripped, so that a name present only in a docstring is reported as prose-only. Module names count as live. `research/code/tests/test_doc_cites_live_declarations.py` runs the check as a test and fails when a listed document cites a name the repository does not define, or when a listed document is missing. The documents it reads are those in the list `DOCS` of the certify script.

The check proves that a citation resolves, not that the declaration says what the document says; the (sig) marks above record which statements are read against signatures. It cannot see Mathlib, so a Mathlib lemma no proof here uses would be reported dead; this file cites none.

## Paper theorems and their declarations

The numbered theorems of the paper (the v3 sources under `research/paper/v3/`) and the declarations that carry them. Prefix `MassGap.` omitted; where a theorem has several declarations the table names the main ones, and the per-section maps `research/paper/v3/*.lean-map.md`, the working source of this table, list them all with their open hypotheses. "None" means no hypothesis the library leaves unproved; the remaining hypotheses are the conditions stated in the theorem. $\mathrm{CS}(N)$ is `ClayStatementG.ClayStatementOf (SU N)`; $\mathrm{CS}$ is `ClayStatement`, that is $\forall N\ge2$, $\mathrm{CS}(N)$.

| Theorem | Result | Lean declaration | Open hypotheses |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | the reduction of $\mathrm{CS}(N)$ to (S), (M), (E) | `OneInput.clayStatement_of_strongEnd` (conclusion `ClayStatementG.ClayStatementOf (SU N)`) | (S) `CouplingTower.StrongEnd N`; (M) `MeasuredTower.MeasTowerG (SU N)` along every tower; (E) `ConstantPhysicsLine.ExistAlong` |
| 2.1 | the $SU(2)$ step and its four properties | `SU2Quat.det_ne_zero_of_dominant`, `SU2Quat.IsQuat.normalise_mem`, `BlockSU2.blockLink`, `BlockSU2.continuous_blockLink`, `blockLink_gauge`, `blockLink_inv`, `blockLink_perm` | none |

## Declaration lists by result

The long record of the development (archived with the release) ends each of its sections with a list of the declarations behind that section. The lists are given here so that every name the paper leaves out can be found. The theorem numbers inside these lists are the long record's own; the paper's sections for them are given in each heading, and the table above supersedes the numbers.
